    because subdivision M 1's grant is enumerated and *"complete application"* confines what the Board
    may require. **Trace it:** *"earliest qualifying retirement date"* runs from the Board crediting
    *"all of the service credit purchased"*, and there is none to credit; so there is no **final**
    qualifying retirement date, **subsection F's duty to retire has no deadline**, and subdivision D 1's
    § 51.1-153 notice never triggers; so no ground in subdivision **D 7** releases the reservation; so
    the reservation sits against D 2's $3,000,000 indefinitely, and subdivision D 3 then requires a fresh
    DPB confirmation *"for each fiscal year in which any reservation remains in effect"* and bars
    acceptance of any further election until it is obtained. **The certified subsidy is positive, not
    zero**, because its *"shall not be less than"* limb is a **floor and not a ceiling**: the timing
    effect between the two valuation limbs does not vanish when the quantity does. **Who bears it:** the
    applicants on the subdivision **D 5** waiting list, whose remedy is express — D 3 makes a waiting-list
    placement *"a determination of the Board for the purposes of subsection M"* and M 2 preserves Article
    5 APA review. **They are not in the stakeholder map as bearers of this cost, and that is routed to
    the Stakeholder Analyst rather than drafted here.**

    **Three routes to that state. Store revision 41 closes one, and the correction against the reviewer
    is that its sentence does not close the other two.** Route 1, **pre-acceptance**: clause (ii) yields
    zero or no value. **Closed** — a determinate zero means the member has reached her unreduced date at
    the filing date, so clause (v) fails and she is not an eligible member; and where clause (ii) has no
    value, subsection C's new no-value sentence says the limb does not limit. Route 2, **post-acceptance**:
    subdivision D 6's second sentence clause (i) lets the member *"reduce the amount of service credit to
    be purchased, subject to subsection C"*, and nothing sets a floor above zero. Route 3,
    **post-acceptance**: subdivision **B 4 f clause (i) of § 51.1-142.2** lets her reduce to *"the
    largest amount for which the Board may accept payment"*, which is **zero** where the Board determines
    under § 415(n) that there is no limitation year in which it may accept any amount — and B 4 f then
    confines her to clause (i) or clause (iii), so electing clause (i) is rational if clause (iii)
    cancellation forecloses her under subsection H. **Pass 13's recommended sentence — *"The Board shall
    not accept an election under which no service credit is to be purchased."* — is keyed to
    acceptance, and routes 2 and 3 operate after acceptance. It closes route 1, which store revision 41
    has already closed on a ground that does not re-create option (a)'s double screen.**

    **(a) The acceptance limb alone, as a backstop.** One sentence at the end of subsection C. Take it
    only if you do **not** take store revision 41's no-value sentence, and note what it needs: a refusal
    under it is **not** a determination that the applicant is not an eligible member, so subdivision
    D 4's notice does not fire on it and the sentence needs its own notice limb or it reproduces the
    defect the deleted bar had.

    > <u>The Board shall not accept an election under which no service credit is to be purchased.</u>

    **(b) Route the post-acceptance zeros into the machinery that already exists. I recommend this. It
    reaches the two post-acceptance routes — ~~all three routes~~, as this item first claimed, is
    corrected in the addendum below.** Subdivision D 6's fourth sentence already cancels the election,
    refunds every amount paid, releases the reservation and requires notice. Widening its trigger costs
    one clause and adds no provision.

    > In subdivision D 6, in the fourth sentence, for *"reduces the amount of service credit the member
    > may purchase under clause (ii) of subsection C to zero"* read <u>"reduces the amount of service
    > credit the member may purchase under clause (ii) of subsection C to zero, or where the service
    > credit to be purchased under an election is reduced to zero under clause (i) of this
    > subdivision or under clause (i) of subdivision B 4 f of § 51.1-142.2"</u>.

    **(c) Do nothing.** Defensible only on frequency: route 1 is closed, and routes 2 and 3 each require
    a member to choose a zero purchase over cancellation. **It is not defensible if anyone is asked
    whether the bill says what happens**, because the answer is that the reservation never expires and
    D 3 then stops the programme for everyone behind her.

    **What I need from Fiscal before (c) can be priced, and I am not asserting it.** The authorised basis
    contains no § 415(n) member with no acceptable limitation year that I can see, but the engine is not
    mine and the frequency of route 3 is the only thing that makes (c) arguable. **Figures: (a) and (b)
    move no figure on the authorised basis** — both operate on an election already accepted or about to
    be, and the admitted population is fixed by clauses (v) to (viii) and the $170,000 ceiling. **(b)
    pairs with item 34 item 5**, which gives that notice its deadline and its standard-cost figure; take
    both and the zero case has one route, one cancellation, one notice and one review.

71. **P14-9 — the measuring point of the 12-month cap on the tolling date-setter. NEW. Mine, raised rather
    than resolved, and the reviewer's own fix direction does not work.** The date-setter in the definition
    of *"final qualifying retirement date"* gives the member, where tolling has prevented her from
    retiring, a date two months after the Board last acts on her request — *"and in no event later than 12
    months after the latest date the preceding sentence permits,"* which is the outer bound. The only route
    into the sentence is the Board over-running its own 90-day reconsideration deadline, and **nothing in
    the section bounds that over-run.** So the longer the Board takes, the less the sentence gives her, and
    past about 21 months it gives her nothing: outer bound 1 March 2033, request filed 1 February 2033,
    Board acts 1 September 2035, and she is capped at 1 March 2034 — eighteen months before the Board
    decides the very question her retirement turns on. That is the structural harm pass 12's F-1 condemned:
    the protective branch should never shorten the ordinary window.

    **The reviewer's fix is surplusage and I did not enact it.** It proposes *"in no event later than 12
    months after the first day of the second month following the month in which the Board last so acts"* —
    which bounds the sentence's own date by itself plus twelve months, so the cap could never bind.
    §6.0-43 item 4.

    **There is no middle option, and that is the point: the cap is the occupancy bound.** Her date is what
    releases the reservation, because G 2 matures 60 days after it. Any form that never cuts her off makes
    occupancy a function of Board delay.

    **(a) Leave it as drafted.** Occupancy per election is bounded on the face of the text at the outer
    bound plus 12 months. The cost is the member above: she loses the window for the Board's delay.

    **(b) Never cut her off. I lean to this and I will not enact it, because it unbounds occupancy.** One
    clause, and it leaves the cap doing its work in every case where the Board acts in time.

    > In the definition of *"final qualifying retirement date"*, for *"and in no event later than 12 months
    > after the latest date the preceding sentence permits"* read <u>"and in no event later than 12 months
    > after the latest date the preceding sentence permits, except that where the Board acts upon the
    > request after that date the member's final qualifying retirement date is the first day of the second
    > month following the month in which the Board last so acts"</u>.

    **(c) Bound the Board's delay instead of her window.** Give M 2's 90 days a consequence. **I advise
    against the obvious form** — a deemed decision — for the reason §7 item 33 was withdrawn: § 2.2-4021 B
    already supplies a statutory default decision in the member's favour by the Article 3 route, and
    layering a second one on a capped benefit puts a $170,000 reservation into existence by operation of
    law against limitations D 2 calls *"self-executing."*

    **Figures: (a) moves nothing. (b) adds reservation occupancy in the tail of the Board-delay
    distribution and nothing at the central case**, because no central-case member reaches the sentence at
    all — it requires the Board to breach its own 90 days. Routed to Fiscal as a tail assumption, not as a
    reprice.

    **ADDENDUM, STORE REVISION 47 — pass 15's S-2 is this same question asked of the *rescission*
    date-setter, and it is now §7 item 85.** The two caps have the same structural problem and the answer
    should be one answer: a 12-month cap measured from any date earlier than the Board's own act either
    binds and names a date in the past or is subordinated and never binds. **Store revision 47 made the
    rescission cap determinate and did not make it a bound, and says so.** If you answer this item and
    item 85 the same way, one of the two edits becomes unnecessary and I will cut it.

72. **P14-10 — which reading of G 4's clock to enact. NEW. This is the one pass 14 put to you, and it is
    the only one of the four that is not mine.** G 4's clock runs from *"the later of the last day on which
    the member may retire in a qualifying retirement **under this subdivision** and the member's final
    qualifying retirement date, if any."* Pass 12 read the first limb as the ninetieth-day date, and that
    was right on the text as it then stood. Store revision 39's own proviso and one-extension cap have made
    it ambiguous, because a retirement on the ninetieth-day date is a *qualifying retirement* only if her
    final date has moved to reach it — and whether it has now depends on the proviso and the cap.

    **It is a fiscal ambiguity and not a member-protection one. Both readings are safe for the member.**
    They differ by about **three months of reservation occupancy per repeat separation**, because on the
    nominal reading the Board's determination, the refund and the release of up to $170,000 plus its
    contingency all slide to a date on which the member cannot in fact retire.

    **(a) Nominal — the phrase means the ninetieth-day date whatever her final date is.** Four words, and
    it is the member-neutral and fiscally worse reading.

    > …*"the last day on which the member may retire in a qualifying retirement under this subdivision*
    > <u>, determined as though the extension provided by this subdivision applied</u>*"*…

    **(b) Actual — the phrase means the last date on which a retirement would in fact qualify.** The
    *"later of"* then does nothing where the extension is switched off, and the reservation clears three
    months earlier.

    > …*"the last day on which the member may retire in a qualifying retirement under this subdivision*
    > <u> or, if earlier, under the definition of "final qualifying retirement date" in subsection A</u>*"*…

    **I have no view on which you should pick** and neither did the reviewer. **One correction to what
    Fiscal has been told:** [`pass12-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-pass12-disposition) §6 says
    F-22 is *"closed by the one-extension cap."* On reading (a) it is closed as to the **window** and open
    as to **occupancy**, and the Modeler should be told which reading is enacted.

    **ADDENDUM, STORE REVISION 47 — pass 15's m-7, an adjacent ambiguity, not a new question.** G 4
    provides that a member's final qualifying retirement date *"shall not be extended under this
    subdivision by reason of more than one involuntary separation."* The rescission date-setter is keyed to
    a **rescission**, not to a separation, so on the face of it the one-extension cap does not reach it,
    though in substance the movement is by reason of a separation. **Bounded — at most one further movement
    of about four months.** One clause settles it either way — *"and the preceding sentence does not limit
    this sentence"*, or the converse — and whichever reading you take on this item, the drafting answer
    should be express rather than left to construction.

73. **P14-8 — restore the nullity with the machinery it needs, or keep the cure store revision 43 enacted? **ANNOTATED IN STORE REVISION 50.** Pass 16's R-4 is an addendum here and **new §7 item 100** carries the half of it that is a money provision. One point bears directly on the nullity question: store revision 47's E5, which replaced the nullity with a cure, **removed the only outer bound on how long a retirement allowance may lawfully be withheld** in the late-notice case — 120 days before, unbounded now. **If you restore the nullity, store revision 50's R-4 edit falls away with it**, as does part of item 100.
    NEW. Mine, and the direction of effect runs against the member.** Store revision 39 made a G 2
    determination that takes effect before the end of the member's notice period *"of no effect"*. **The
    section supplies no route to undo what the Board has already done on the strength of it** — within 60
    days it has refunded the member and her employer, and it has released a reservation that is now
    reserved for the next applicant off the waiting list. G 4's reinstatement sentence does not reach her,
    because that release **was** in accordance with D 7. So on a three-day mailing miscount her credit was
    never reduced, her reservation was never released, $150,000 of it is reserved for somebody else, and
    the aggregate charged exceeds $3,000,000 — an unfunded liability flowing into the employer contribution
    rate under art. X, § 11's actuarial-methods clause. **It is not an art. X, § 7 defect and no document
    of ours may concede one:** no money leaves the treasury without an appropriation.

    **Enacted (edit 9):** the determination *"takes effect instead on the first day on which it may take
    effect under this sentence."* Her 30-day floor and her M 2 period are preserved in full; what she loses
    is the windfall.

    **The alternative, drafted and not enacted.** Keep the nullity and give it the three verbs G 4's
    rescission limb has, plus the notices:

    > <u>and a determination that takes effect in contravention of this sentence is of no effect. Where a
    > determination is of no effect under this subdivision, the Board shall restore the service credit
    > reduced under it, shall reinstate the reservation and the contingency amount released on account of
    > it in the manner subdivision G 4 requires in the case of a reservation released otherwise than in
    > accordance with subdivision D 7, shall re-apply to the election any amount refunded under clause
    > (ii) of this subdivision to the extent the member or any employer repays it, and shall give the
    > member, any employer that paid on the member's behalf, and each applicant then on the waiting list
    > maintained under subdivision D 5 written notice of the facts and of the reasons, in each case no
    > later than 60 days after the Board determines that the determination is of no effect.</u>

    **What the alternative costs, stated plainly.** It is six lines rather than one, it puts the
    reinstatement over the $3,000,000 by operation of a clerical error, and it triggers G 4's *"accept no
    further election until the excess is eliminated"* — so the **wait-listed applicant** pays for the
    Board's miscount. **I recommend the cure that is enacted**, and the choice is yours because it is the
    one place in this revision where I reduced a protection a review had put in.

    **ADDENDUM, STORE REVISION 47 — two things from pass 15 bear on this item and one of them is a reason
    to read it now.** **m-2:** G 4 keeps *"a determination made in contravention of this sentence is of no
    effect"* for the disability-pending bar, while G 2 now **cures** a premature determination. The bill
    therefore gives two different consequences to two premature determinations under one subsection, and
    the surviving nullity has no more reconciliation machinery than the one it replaced. **Whichever way
    you answer this item, the two should match**, and the matching edit is one sentence in either
    direction. **S-4:** store revision 47 reconciled the cure with G 2's *"in no event later than 120
    days"* outer limit, which the cure did not reach and which carried no consequence. **If you restore the
    nullity, that edit falls away with it** — so answering this item late costs a wasted edit, and
    answering it now costs nothing.

74. **P14-6 — the deeming device for an undetermined involuntary separation. NEW. Mine, declined and
    drafted.** Store revision 43 gives the member an express route — she may file written evidence, and the
    Board must determine within 30 days with notice, reasons and subsection M review — and aligns the
    extension's predicate with the Board's **learning** of the facts, so a Board sitting on the question no
    longer costs her the extension. The reviewer would go one step further and deem the separation
    determined where the Board does not act:

    > <u>and where the Board does not act within that period the separation is deemed to have been
    > determined to have occurred on the date stated in the evidence the member filed</u>

    **Why I have not enacted it.** The deeming device is the section's own — it is used in the two *"of no
    effect"* clauses — but every one of those deems an **act of the Board** ineffective, and this one would
    deem a **fact** found against the party that funds the consequence. With the predicates aligned it adds
    certainty rather than substance. And it is the device §7 item 33 was withdrawn to avoid, pointed the
    other way. **It is one sentence, it costs the member nothing, and if you want certainty over
    conservatism it is the right provision. Figures: it moves occupancy in the tail only**, and only for
    members whose agency failed to file and whose Board failed to determine.


75. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 44, from [MOO-121](/MOO/issues/MOO-121)'s M2 and from the item I had carried
    myself. Subdivision D 7 releases a reserved subsidy on six events and all six presuppose an election
    the Board has already accepted — so the money an unaccepted application parks is freed by nothing.**
    Store revision 44 enacts the Director's §7 item 34 item 3, which adds the **refusal notices** of
    subsection C and subdivision D 2 as release events. Four cases are still not reached: the applicant
    refused under **D 4** as not an eligible member; the applicant who **withdraws**, which subdivision
    D 3 expressly contemplates; the applicant who **dies before acceptance**, G 4 reaching death only
    *"after the Board accepts the election"*; and the applicant who never files the **written
    acknowledgment** the sentence of subdivision D 1 beginning *"The Board shall not accept an election until the member has filed"* makes a condition of acceptance **(ordinal corrected in store revision 59: it was D 1's fourth sentence when this item was written, is now its fifth, and the item previously said "third")**, for which the bill sets no
    deadline at all. **The exposure is the cap's, not the member's:** each parked application holds up to
    $170,000 plus the 20 percent contingency — **$204,000** — against the $3,000,000, and on MOO-121's
    arithmetic fourteen silent applicants would hold the whole appropriation. One clause, drafted:

    > <u>The Board shall release a reserved certified subsidy and its contingency amount upon giving the
    > notice required by subsection C or by subdivision D 2 or D 4, upon the withdrawal of the
    > application, or upon the death of the applicant before the Board accepts the election.</u>

    That replaces the sentence this revision enacted and reaches three of the four cases. **The fourth
    cannot be cured by a release event at all**: the applicant who never files D 1's written
    acknowledgment leaves no event to key to, so what she needs is a **deadline** — *"no later than 30
    days after the Board gives the certification"*, with the application treated as withdrawn if it
    passes — and that is a duty on the applicant, which is why it is on your card and not in the text.

    **I have not enacted it**, because it names release events the commission did not, and a release is a
    decision about whose reservation is freed and when. **It cannot lower the count** — a released
    reservation can only admit a later applicant — and it is the cheapest unforced error in subsection D
    if it is left.

76. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 44. MOO-98's M1 is enacted in form (B) on your answer that item 35 (a)
    governs, and two words of it are mine rather than Red Team's.** Subsection C now carries the
    consequence D 1, D 3, D 4 and G 4 each carry for the same kind of delay — a line in the subsection J
    report — plus review of the **failure** at any time until the notice is given, and the member's own
    window running from **actual** notice. Two things for you, neither of which blocks anything:

    - **Red Team's words for the second limb are not taken, and the choice is reversible in one
      sentence.** They drafted *"the notice shall be treated as given on the last day of that period for
      the purposes of subsection M"*, which opens the window and **starts its 30 days against a member
      who has received nothing**. Form (B) gives her the same access without the trap. If you prefer the
      deeming, say so and it is one substitution.
    - **MOO-121's M5 is cured by an amendment to M 2 that is wider than MOO-98's finding, and I am
      naming it.** Form (B) confers review of a *failure to act*, and M 2's gateway ran only to *"a
      determination of the Board."* A right into a gateway that excludes it is the F4 defect displaced
      from the class to the subject matter, so M 2's first sentence now reaches *"a failure of the Board
      to make a determination or to give a notice within a period required by this section"* and supplies
      the window for it. **That reaches every period in the section, not only subsection C's** — it is
      the general form of the cure and the only one that does not leave the same hole next door. One
      clause out and form (B)'s review limb is hollow again.

77. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 44, and it is one word.** Your parity answer put subdivision D 2's refusal
    notice on the clock subsection C's notice uses: *"no later than 30 days after **the member's**
    application is completed."* The rest of that sentence names *"the applicant"* — twice — because the
    refusal happens before acceptance. **I enacted your words unchanged rather than conforming them**,
    since you chose the string on the card and the clock is keyed to the same fact either way. For
    *"the member's application"* read *"the applicant's application"* and the sentence names one class
    throughout. **No date and no duty moves; it removes an argument that the two words mean two people.**

78. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 44, from [MOO-121](/MOO/issues/MOO-121)'s S4 and M6, and it is a money
    question about a sentence that is already enacted — store revision 41's no-value sentence of subsection
    C.** That sentence says clause (ii) **does not limit** a member for whom no number of months answers
    it. MOO-121 measured who that leaves uncapped and the answer is specific: **all 22 live cells are
    Plan 1 members aged 49.9167 — one month short of fifty — with 27.42, 28.42 or 29.42 years.** Their
    unreduced date is their fiftieth-birthday month, so no quantity of service reaches it at the filing
    date, and clause (vi) admits them because that date falls in the month after filing.

    | service at filing | shortfall at the earliest reachable date | purchase as the text stands | overshoot |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | 29.4167 yrs | 6 months | 3.0000 yrs | **6.0x** |
    | 28.4167 yrs | 18 months | 3.0000 yrs | **2.0x** |
    | 27.4167 yrs | 30 months | 3.0000 yrs | **1.2x** |

    **She cannot buy less, because the certification states the quantity and no limb caps it — so 69
    percent of that class is refused at the $170,000 ceiling for buying credit it does not need**
    (0.0567 of 0.0826 expected participants), against 6.29 percent for the cohort as a whole. **The sign
    is the opposite of the intuitive one:** capping the quantity *admits more members and raises
    utilisation*, because members pushed over the ceiling by a purchase they do not need come back under
    it — 18.3965 to **18.4172** participants, 78.1872 to **78.2929** percent, both inside the precision
    the package publishes. The capped alternate, one clause:

    > <u>…the amount determined under this clause is the number of months of creditable service the
    > member needs in order to have reached that date as of the earliest date on which any amount of
    > creditable service would have caused the member to have reached it.</u>

    **I have not enacted it, and I will not resolve it by drafting choice.** Store revision 41 enacted
    the present reading deliberately, on your own ground that *"a count may not travel on a basis the
    statute does not require"*, and after [MOO-119](/MOO/issues/MOO-119) measured the same class. Two
    passes now disagree about the same sentence and the disagreement is about **who bears the overshoot**,
    not about what the words mean. **Two things to weigh:** the containment is **demographic, not
    statutory** — nothing in the text bounds the class, and on the measured per-member means a one-third
    share of admitted elections in it would take utilisation to about 90 percent; and the alternate also
    restores D 6's fourth sentence for the class, which cannot fire while clause (ii) has no value
    (MOO-121's **M6**).

79. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 44, from [MOO-121](/MOO/issues/MOO-121)'s S5 and M8. *"Applicant"* is
    undefined, it now carries a right, and the definition I had proposed would have re-created the very
    defect store revision 44 cured.** My proposal read *"**a member** who has filed an application"*.
    MOO-84's F4 holding is that an applicant refused **because she is not a member** is exactly who
    subdivision D 4's promise addresses and exactly who M 2 excluded — so that definition hands the
    teacher a D 4 notice promising a review she cannot have, one definition later. The corrected form:

    > <u>"Applicant" means a person who has filed an application for an election under this section,
    > whether or not the application is complete, whether or not the person is a member, and whether or
    > not the person is an eligible member.</u>

    **The weight is larger than I stated.** Counted in the live operative text, *"applicant"* has **26**
    uses in subdivisions D 2 to D 5 (21 bare, 5 possessive) and **30** in the operative text as a whole,
    four of them outside subdivision D — three in subsection A's giving-of-notice paragraph and one in
    G 4's waiting-list notice. **Still proposed and not drafted**, because a definition that reaches 30
    operative uses is not a conforming amendment; it is a decision about who the section speaks to.

80. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 44. The residues MOO-121 found that I did not cure, each with the reason,
    none of them worth a revision of its own.**

    - **M7 — the sunset continues the Board's half of the post-retirement-employment guardrail and not
      the agency's.** Enactment 3 clause (i) now expressly continues the I 2 notice and determination for
      accepted elections, but I 2's other duty — a **state agency** reporting a re-engagement within 30
      days — is the agency's, and clause (i) continues only the Board's duties and the member's rights.
      G 4's involuntary-separation agency notice is in the same position. The programme concentrates
      retirements at its own sunset, so this is the cohort it bites. **Answer it with item 66**, which is
      the member-side form of the same question; one phrase in clause (i) (*"the Board of Trustees **and
      every state agency**"*) does both.
    - **m2 — edit 10 does not say that the standard cost of a portion of a year is a proportionate
      part.** *"Each year or portion thereof"* is a round-up idiom. Left as it is because the phrase is
      lifted verbatim from subdivision B 4 a's own definition and from § 51.1-142.2 B 3, so it carries the
      chapter's settled usage; the cheap cure keys it to subsection F of § 51.1-142.2, which this bill
      already uses for partial-month valuation in G 3 and G 4.
    - **m3 — the D 4 notice's standard-cost content is now required in two places.** Subdivision D 4
      already states it; D 5's third sentence as amended now states it too. Harmless as drafted; the risk
      is divergence if either is amended alone.
    - **m4 — D 3's borrowed D 5 notice says the wrong thing, and it is pre-existing.** D 3's
      insufficient-appropriation branch sends the applicant *"the notice required by subdivision 5"*,
      whose content is *"that fact"* — insufficient **headroom** under the $3,000,000, not insufficient
      **appropriation**. One clause fixes it whenever D 3 is next opened.
    - **D 6's fourth sentence is keyed to clause (ii) alone.** A recertification that reduces the
      purchasable amount to zero through clause (i) or clause (iii) falls between sentence 4 (not clause
      (ii)) and sentence 5 (*"but not to zero"*). Pre-existing, one sentence, and it belongs with item 78.
    - **The clause (vii) calendar residue, which Fiscal and I agree is below publishable precision.**
      Clause (vii)'s corresponding-date month makes the test a comparison of two dates, so it never counts
      months and *"at least one month earlier"* is inclusive — which is why 18.3965, 78.1872 percent and
      $1,321,112 stand. The residue is that a corresponding-date month varies in length with the calendar:
      31 days from a 31 March date, 28 from a 1 March one. It moves a fraction of the **0.5828** expected
      elections that sit at exactly one month of advance, inside a count published at two significant
      figures. **No edit, and no figure to restate.**


    ---

    **STORE REVISION 46 — pass 14's Q5 re-traced the routes and found one this item did not have, and
    store revision 44's restoration of the zero bar has moved a second. My claim that option (b)
    *"reaches all three routes"* was true of this item's own taxonomy and is false of the full set; it is
    corrected in the lead of (b) above, and (b)'s citation is re-cut.** The route this item did not have
    is **a member whose cap is positive and whose election is for nothing**: nothing in the bill requires
    the elected quantity to exceed zero, subdivision D 4's acceptance duty is mandatory, clause (vii)
    tests her **maximum purchasable amount** and not what she elects, and **the restored zero bar does
    not reach her either**, being keyed to the clause (ii) **amount**. **Option (b) as re-cut reaches the
    two post-acceptance routes; option (a) reaches that one; neither reaches all three.** The citation
    re-cut matters on its own: the member's **permission** to reduce is in D 6's second sentence, but the
    reduction is **given effect** by D 6's sixth sentence, which names it *"under clause (i) of this
    subdivision or under clause (i) of subdivision B 4 f of § 51.1-142.2"* — and without the bill's own
    words for the same act, (b) left that route open.

    **There is now an option (d): take (a) and (b) together, with a notice limb for (a). It is the only
    combination that reaches all three routes and it is what I recommend if you want this closed in one
    answer.** I have not drafted it as pass 14's single sentence, for a drafting reason rather than a
    preference: one sentence would have to duplicate D 6's fourth-sentence remedy in a second provision
    or cross-cite it by sentence ordinal, and this file has had three sentence ordinals rot. **One thing
    to know before taking it:** (d)'s refusal limb speaks to the **elected quantity** and the restored
    bar speaks to the **cap**, so the two are disjoint and should be cut as one sentence with two limbs
    rather than as two sentences a reader will try to reconcile. **Figures: none of (a) to (d) moves
    one.** **The four-route table, the mapping to both taxonomies, the verbatim text of (d) and the two
    further cautions are at [`moo123-pass14-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-87#document-moo123-pass14-disposition)
    §7 question 4.**

81. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 46, from [MOO-123](/MOO/issues/MOO-123)'s pass 14, finding M-3. Subsection C's duty to *state* the amounts in the certification still has no *or else*, although store revision 44 gave its determination and notice duties one. 16 characters closes it.** Pass 14 tested five routes and found all five closed; store revision 44's two new sentences then closed four of them, for failures to **make a determination** and to **give a notice**. **A certification is neither** in this section's vocabulary, so the one duty with no consequence is the one pass 14 named. **(a) Widen store revision 44's own reporting sentence to reach a failure *"to state a matter"* — recommended, 16 characters, no new sentence, no new register, no new review route. (b) Put the subsection C amounts in subdivision D 1's content list, which makes acceptance turn on a clerical omission. (c) Leave it unenforced and say so in §6.** The operative effect — clause (ii) does not limit — is self-executing either way; what the gap costs is the **record**, not the member's cap and not a dollar. **No figure moves on any option. Drafted in full, with the verbatim text of (a) and (b) and the nullity option I am not proposing, at [`moo123-pass14-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-87#document-moo123-pass14-disposition) §7 question 1.**

82. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 46, from pass 14's M-1 and M-2 together with [MOO-124](/MOO/issues/MOO-124)'s answer. The base on which this bill measures a member's service is stated in clause (ii) of subsection C and in clause (v) of *"eligible member"*, is absent from clauses (vi) to (viii) — the three limbs that actually compute the unreduced retirement eligibility date — and the words were mine twice over and were never agreed. This is the same phrase §7 item 43 asks you about and the two should be answered together.** **M-2:** *expressio unius* now runs against the load-bearing limbs, and a uniform insertion will not work, because clause (viii) is keyed to a future date. **M-1:** a narrower base makes a member's date recede and can **admit**, its vehicle being the no-cost armed-forces credit under subsection I of § 51.1-142.2, and the containment is clause (vii) reading her **full** record — which works only because of the asymmetry M-2 complains of. **MOO-124, and this is the fact that decides it:** *"creditable service"* is *"prior service as set forth in § 51.1-142.2 plus membership service for which credit is allowable"*, and the prior-service limb is a **purchase** statute, so *"entitled to credit"* may **sweep in prior service the member could buy and has not bought** — on which reading clause (v) excludes the entire population this bill is written for. The chapter's two registers are *"allowable"* for credit not yet given and *"credited with"* for credit actually given. **Five options, (a) to (e), drafted verbatim with the figures and the one I have to tell you is dangerous, at [`moo123-pass14-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-87#document-moo123-pass14-disposition) §7 question 2. I recommend (b) — the chapter's giving register, stated once in subsection A — and I have enacted nothing**, because the base is the bill's only quantity provision and its sole eligibility screen, the words in it are mine and nobody has chosen them, and every option has a direction of effect on admissions the authorised basis cannot represent.

83. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 46, from pass 14's M-5. A Board error that turns the no-limit trigger *on* raises the member's cap, nothing in the bill lets her take the benefit, and the only exit may bar her from ever applying again.** Subdivision D 6 reaches a change in **either** direction and the Board must recertify, but the member's options are one-way: D 6's second sentence clause (i) permits her only to *"reduce the amount of service credit to be purchased"*, and **nothing permits an increase.** To re-apply she must cancel under subdivision G 5, which credits her with the service credit for which full payment has been made — and **subsection H** then bars a member *"who has been credited with service credit under subdivision G 2, G 3, or G 5"* from making another election. **Whether a member credited with nothing has been "credited with service credit" is not resolved anywhere in this bill.** **Two independent edits, both recommended and neither enacted: (i) an increase limb in D 6, with a 30-day deadline and an insufficiency limb in the *"of no effect"* register this bill uses in G 2 and G 4; and (ii) three lines in subsection H resolving the credited-with-nothing question, which reaches every cancellation under G 5 before any payment and so outlives this finding.** Direction of effect on (i) is **upward** by the difference between the certified and the recertified cap, for a class I cannot size, because the Board's error rate is not in the authorised basis and should not be invented. **Both drafted verbatim at [`moo123-pass14-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-87#document-moo123-pass14-disposition) §7 question 3.**


84. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 47, from [MOO-127](/MOO/issues/MOO-127)'s pass 15, finding S-1 — the most serious finding in the pass, and the choice in it is yours because I have already closed the money exposure with the cheaper half.** Store revision 43's settlement sentence for the member who never retires sits **outside** G 4's *"the earliest of"* sentence and carried no exclusion from it, so the same certified subsidy could be settled twice — once when her date passed and again on her death — and **subsection N pays on each**, up to **$170,000** twice on one election. **Store revision 47 enacts the bar: *"and no certified subsidy is settled more than once under this subdivision or under subdivision 1."*** The exposure is closed and nothing waits on you. **What is open is whether to replace the bar with the structure.** **(a) Keep the bar — enacted, 14 words, no sentence moves.** **(b) Make the settlement date clause (d) of *"the earliest of"*, carrying its own condition** — *"(d) the member's final qualifying retirement date or, where the member has none, the completion date stated in the certification, where by that date the member has neither died nor been approved for a disability retirement allowance nor retired"* — on which a second settlement is **structurally impossible** rather than barred, and the clause (iii) member who later retires cannot settle on the earlier of two dates. The reviewer prefers (b) and so would I on a file with room; it is longer, it rewrites a sentence passes 12 to 15 have certified, and **it changes which date governs a member who retires after her date passed, which nobody has been asked about**. **No figure moves on either.** Drafted verbatim at [`pass15-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-pass15-disposition) §5 question 1. **ANNOTATED IN STORE REVISION 50 — pass 16's R-6 is the evidence you asked for, and the answer to your question is yes: the bar fails in two identified places where clause (d) would not** (the rescission limb's reinstatement duty, whose only deadline was the settlement date, and the recertification-before-redetermination sequencing duty). **Store revision 50 has given both provisions data that do not depend on a settlement occurring, so option (a) no longer carries the defect** — your choice is now purely structural. Detail at [`pass16-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-pass16-disposition) §3, R-6.

85. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 47, from pass 15's S-2, and it belongs with item 71 because it is the same occupancy choice.** The rescission limb's 12-month cap measured from *"the date that would have applied under this subdivision had the Board determined the separation on the date it learned of it"* — a counterfactual that is **inert** and that, in the ordinary case, **has no referent at all**. Store revision 47 gives it the **separation date** as its datum and subordinates it expressly to the rescission-based date, so no reading now re-strips a member of four years of credit the moment it is restored. **That removes an undefined operative term and it does not create a bound: a cap measured from any date before the rescission either binds and names a date in the past, or never binds. The reviewer's own proposed wording has the same property.** So the real question is the one the text cannot answer: **how long may a reservation sit against the $3,000,000 on a rescission the Board may make at any time?** **(a) Accept it and disclose** — the member is protected, occupancy is bounded only by the Board's act, and the disclosure goes in the fiscal statement. **(b) Bar a rescission where the Board learns of the separation more than [24] months after it** — a real bound, and it strips the member whose agency never filed and who never knew the right existed, which is the class edits 8 and 14 of store revision 43 exist for. **(c) Keep the rescission and bound the *charge*** — release the reservation on the determination and reinstate only to the extent the appropriation then permits, which puts the cost on the member instead of the waiting list. **I recommend (a) and I will not enact it**, because it is an appropriation-occupancy choice. Drafted at [`pass15-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-pass15-disposition) §5 question 2. **ANNOTATED IN STORE REVISION 50.** Pass 16 **confirms** the inertness reasoning and supplies a better ground — the cap is measured from a date that always lies in the past — so **the cap is deleted in store revision 50 on R-14**, which removes nothing real and leaves this item as the live occupancy choice. **R-19 corrects the sentence this item's recommendation partly rested on:** option (c) bounds the **charge**, which is a real bound that strips no member, so this is a choice between three real bounds with different victims. Two addenda, neither a new fight: **R-11** (the clause-(ii) extension chain escapes subdivision B 4 f's 24-month cap) and **R-12** (E5 and E13 compound to widen the rescission class). **No figure is asserted by either and I move none.** Store revision 50's R-13 edit and its R-1 floor each lengthen occupancy, each disclosed and each reversible in one sentence.

86. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 47, from pass 15's S-6 and M-11 together. The reviewer answered the question you asked at item 74 — does declining the deeming rule leave the member without a remedy — and the answer is yes, for a reason pass 14 did not give, with a fix that costs you nothing on the principle you objected to.** Store revision 43 gave the member a filing, a 30-day determination, notice, reasons and subsection M review. **What it did not give her: her final qualifying retirement date keeps running while the Board sits, because an evidence filing is neither of M 2's two tolling triggers; and if the Board simply does not determine, there is no *"case decision"* within § 2.2-4001, so § 2.2-4026 gives her nothing to appeal.** Her remedy is mandamus, at her cost, on a clock that is still running. **Two sentences, both drafted, and neither deems a fact against the payer:** **(i)** add the filing to M 2's tolling triggers, which also brings it inside M 2's own 120-day aggregate cap; and **(ii)** M-11's *"A member may file such evidence once in respect of any separation,"* which closes the repeat-filing tail the reviewer routed to Fiscal — M 2's cap bounds the window, so what repeats is Board workload and reservation occupancy, not the window. **I recommend both. I have enacted neither**, because (i) is a new tolling trigger and (ii) takes a filing right away. **Item 74 stays open and this is the better answer to it.** Drafted at [`pass15-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-pass15-disposition) §5 question 3.

87. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 47, from pass 15's M-8, and it is the sharp edge of the disclosure already on your list at item 68's neighbourhood: the Program-wide acceptance freeze lifts if the member fails and is permanent if she succeeds.** A reinstatement over the cap charges the subsidy and its contingency *"notwithstanding that the amount available to be reserved is thereby exceeded"* and the Board *"shall accept no further election until the excess is eliminated."* If she then **retires in a qualifying retirement**, G 1 releases the contingency and any reduction on redetermination — and **subdivision D 2's second sentence keeps the settled amount charged**, so the excess is not eliminated and the freeze never lifts. If she **fails** to retire, subdivision 2 releases the reservation and the freeze lifts. On a $150,000 subsidy the reinstatement creates a $180,000 excess; her retirement clears $30,000 of it; **$150,000 of excess persists and no further election is accepted for the rest of the Program.** **The better outcome for the member is the worse outcome for every wait-listed applicant.** **(a) Exclude a reinstated amount that has been settled from the freeze test** — one clause, drafted; it means the Program keeps accepting against an aggregate that exceeds $3,000,000 on the reservation basis. **(b) Disclose it** and leave the text. **I am neutral and I will not enact either**, because (a) relaxes the one self-executing limitation in the bill and (b) is a fiscal-statement choice. Drafted at [`pass15-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-pass15-disposition) §5 question 4.

88. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 48, from [MOO-128](/MOO/issues/MOO-128)'s pass 15, finding S-3. M 2's final sentence promises the member a judicial remedy § 2.2-4026 will not supply, and Title 51.1 has no general APA hook to fall back on.** Store revision 44 widened M 2 to reach *"a failure of the Board to make a determination or to give a notice within a period required by this section"* and promised Article 5 review to a member aggrieved *"by such a failure."* **A savings clause cannot create jurisdiction:** § 2.2-4001 confines a *"case decision"* to *"any agency proceeding or determination"* and § 2.2-4026 A gives the court action only to a party aggrieved by one — **a failure to determine is neither**. Pass 15 verified all four citations and found **no general APA-review provision in Title 51.1**; § 51.1-124.11 is *"Recovery of payments procured by fraud."* **Four options drafted verbatim at [`moo128-pass15-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-87#document-moo128-pass15-disposition) — (a) to (c) at §5 and (d) at §8. I have enacted none and I now recommend (d). My recommendation of (c) is WITHDRAWN IN STORE REVISION 53, on [MOO-139](/MOO/issues/MOO-139)'s findings, and the reason is below.** **(a)** deem the failure a determination — the reviewer's recommendation, one clause, a device already in the bill; **I recommend against it**, because characterising Board inaction as a case decision opens § 2.2-4021's statutory default, so a member refused by the $170,000 ceiling could obtain a reservation by the Board's silence against limitations subdivision D 2 calls *"self-executing"* — the ground on which §6.4 withdrew an equivalent sentence at pass 9, and which pass 15 did not reach. **(b)** delete *"or by such a failure"*: two words, honest, and less than the bill now promises. **(c)** replace M 2's final sentence with the § 51.1-124.11 formula **already drafted at §6.4** — express notice, an opportunity to be heard under the Administrative Process Act, and an express Article 5 entitlement **for determinations**, saying nothing about failures. It does (b)'s work, replaces a savings clause with a real right, characterises nothing as a case decision, and was recommended here before S-3 was raised and for an independent reason. **One gap: it does not foreclose a § 2.2-4025 A (ii) *"internal management or routine"* argument — but neither does the present sentence.** [MOO-139](/MOO/issues/MOO-139) **has answered that question — § 51.1-124.11's rights ARE textually tied to fraud recovery — and that is what moves my recommendation to (d).** **No figure moves on any of the four.**

    **OPTION (d), ADDED IN STORE REVISION 53 FROM [MOO-139](/MOO/issues/MOO-139), AND IT IS NOW MY
    RECOMMENDATION. Replace M 2's final sentence with the General Assembly's own predicate-creating
    formula, modelled on § 9.1-405, which governs an appeal from a decision of the Virginia Retirement
    System itself:**

    > <u>Upon completion of a reconsideration under this subsection, the final determination issued by
    > the Board shall constitute a case decision as defined in § 2.2-4001. Any member, retiree,
    > beneficiary, or applicant aggrieved by, and claiming the unlawfulness of, such case decision shall
    > have a right to seek judicial review thereof in accordance with Article 5 (§ 2.2-4025 et seq.) of
    > the Administrative Process Act.</u>

    **Optional second limb, and I recommend taking it** — the device §§ 51.1-1135.1 and 51.1-1177 use, in
    this title, for this Board:

    > <u>Articles 3 (§ 2.2-4018 et seq.) and 4 (§ 2.2-4024) of the Administrative Process Act shall not
    > apply to a reconsideration under this subsection.</u>

    **Why (d), and why (c) is withdrawn.** (d) **answers S-3 at the root**: § 2.2-4026 A requires a case
    decision, and (d) *supplies* one by statute instead of assuming it in a savings clause — (b) and (c)
    answer S-3 only by withdrawing the promise. It **reaches the member aggrieved by a failure without
    deeming a non-act to be an act**, because store revision 44 already lets her request reconsideration
    *"in the case of such a failure … at any time before the Board makes the determination or gives the
    notice,"* and under (d) it is the **completed reconsideration** that is the case decision — the whole
    of option (a) without the mechanism that opened § 2.2-4021. The optional limb closes § 2.2-4021 **by
    statute rather than by argument**, which was my objection to (a), and it is not an invention: it is
    the operative sentence of both Title 51.1 appeals sections. **(c) fails on its source text**, which
    MOO-139 read and I had not: § 51.1-124.11's rights are tied to fraud recovery by the demonstrative
    *"such determination"*, whose only antecedent is a determination that a payment was procured by false
    statement; its protected class (*"a member or beneficiary"*) is narrower than M 2's; and its notice and
    hearing run ***"Prior to** making any such determination"* — a **pre-decisional** process for a
    Board-initiated recovery, where reconsideration of a member-initiated determination is
    **post-decisional**. [`pass11-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-pass11-disposition) §179
    withdrew an earlier § 51.1-124.11 substitute for exactly that reason and I did not have it in view.
    **What (d) costs, stated rather than netted away:** the optional limb **removes** Article 3's
    case-decision procedure and Article 4 from the reconsideration, which is a choice about what the
    member gets and it is yours; both Title 51.1 appeals sections pair that disapplication with an
    ERISA-modelled written-notice-and-review floor, and M 2 already supplies a comparable one — a 30-day
    window, written reasons, a 90-day duty to act, and tolling — so I do not think a further floor is
    needed, but the model pairs them and this draft does not. **Without the limb, (d) carries a residual
    § 2.2-4021 question about a reconsideration the Board sits on**, which M 2's own 90-day duty mitigates
    but does not foreclose; that is the one thing I would want counsel to look at before (d) is taken, and
    it is question 6 on [MOO-153](/MOO/issues/MOO-153). **The null result holds and is now checkable:** a
    317-section scan of Title 51.1 found **exactly four** sections carrying any APA, Article 5,
    judicial-review or *"aggrieved"* marker; **§ 51.1-1135.1** and **§ 51.1-1177** are two further Article
    5 provisions, both chapter-confined, so **no general hook arises**, and § 51.1-124.9, the general
    benefit-correction section, carries no procedural rights at all. **M 2 is carrying the whole review
    scheme on its own.** **One precision point for the [Editor](/MOO/agents/editor):** the sentence *"the
    only APA reference in Chapter 1 of Title 51.1 subjects the Board to the APA"* is accurate **only with
    the "Chapter 1" qualifier**, because §§ 51.1-1135.1 and 51.1-1177 are APA references elsewhere in the
    title and they point the other way — **the qualifier must travel with the sentence**. **The
    highest-value remaining gap is for counsel, not research:** whether any Virginia decision has held a
    VRS benefit determination to be a § 2.2-4001 case decision **absent** an express deeming clause. If
    one has, S-3 is narrower than pass 15 and I have both assumed and **(b) becomes more attractive**. **No
    document of ours asserts an answer and none should.**

    **QUESTION 6 IS ANSWERED IN STORE REVISION 56 BY [MOO-153](/MOO/issues/MOO-153), AND THE ANSWER DOES NOT NARROW S-3.** **No Virginia decision has held a VRS benefit determination to be a § 2.2-4001 case decision absent an express deeming clause.** There are four decisions of **unexamined practice** — *Avery* (Va. Ct. App. 2000) and *Retirement System v. Avery*, 262 Va. 538, 551 S.E.2d 612 (2001), which decided only that the appeal was perfected and **expressly did not reach** what constitutes a reviewable case decision; *Cirillo* (2009), the closest thing to an implicit holding because it applied § 2.2-4019(A)(v) and applying Article 3 presupposes a case decision; *Blair* (2015); and *Shelton* (2022). Two structural facts support the assumption — **VRS is not exempt under § 2.2-4002 A or B**, and § 2.2-4001 clause (ii) reaches a determination of compliance with *"any existing requirement for obtaining or retaining a license or other right or benefit"* on its face — and one cuts the other way: **the only express deeming clause in the Code for a VRS determination is § 9.1-405**, confined to the Line of Duty Act. **So (b) does not become more attractive on this ground, and no document of ours asserts an answer — a posture now held on authority rather than on absence of research.** Decision-by-decision table at [`moo153-counsel-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-145#document-moo153-counsel-disposition) §3.

    **Two caveats on (d)'s model, neither previously in this record:** § 9.1-405 **does not disapply any Article of the APA** — its only exclusion is of standing — so it is not authority that a deeming clause can be enacted without Article 3's consequences; and it is Line of Duty Act-specific, so it supports (d)'s **form** and not its **consequences**.

    **MY RECOMMENDATION TO TAKE (d)'s OPTIONAL SECOND LIMB IS WITHDRAWN IN STORE REVISION 56**, on counsel's objection that disapplying Articles 3 and 4 strips the member of the hearing rights this bill exists to confer and is a far larger intervention than the § 2.2-4021 problem needs. I accept it. **(d) without the limb is still my recommendation on this item.** The § 2.2-4021 B problem is answered instead by the narrow carve-out at **item 110** — wanted whichever of (a) to (d) is taken **and even if this item is answered by doing nothing**, because counsel finds the exposure **already live without any of them**. **No option (a) to (d) addresses § 2.2-4021 B; item 110 does.**

    **Pass 14's S-3 scenario pleaded Article 5 review of a failure and that framing is withdrawn**; its administrability grounds stand and edit 1 answers it on those.

    **ANSWERED AND ENACTED IN STORE REVISION 59** — the Policy Director's direction of 12 October 2026, 13:26 UTC. **OPTION (d) WITH THE OPTIONAL SECOND LIMB — the Policy Director's direction, taken over my withdrawn recommendation, and I record the disagreement rather than bury it.** M 2's final sentence is replaced by three: the completed reconsideration *"shall constitute a case decision as defined in § 2.2-4001"*; the aggrieved person *"shall have a right to seek judicial review thereof in accordance with Article 5 (§ 2.2-4025 et seq.)"*; and *"Articles 3 (§ 2.2-4018 et seq.) and 4 (§ 2.2-4024) of the Administrative Process Act shall not apply to a reconsideration under this subsection."* **This answers S-3 at the root** — § 2.2-4026 A needs a case decision and (d) supplies one by statute instead of assuming it in a savings clause.

    **ONE CONFORMING CHANGE TO THE DRAFTED TEXT, AND IT IS NOT OPTIONAL.** Option (d) was drafted in store revision 53 and its class read *"Any member, retiree, beneficiary, or applicant."* **Store revision 58 then widened M 2's class list to six in both of its occurrences** — adding *"personal representative of a deceased member's estate, or other person to whom this section requires a notice to be given"* — and one of those two occurrences **was** the judicial-review sentence (§5.37 records it). Enacting (d) verbatim would have silently taken the Article 5 right away from the rejected claimant and the subdivision B 4 e employer five revisions after store revision 58 gave it to them, which is a policy change nobody directed. **The class enacted is M 2's own six-class list**, and *"Any member"* becomes *"A member"* to match M 2's first sentence. Recorded at §6.0-59 ambiguity 1.

    **WHAT THE LIMB COSTS, STATED AND NOT NETTED AWAY.** Disapplying Article 3 removes § 2.2-4019 informal fact finding and § 2.2-4020 formal hearings from the reconsideration, and Article 4 removes the hearing officer. **Counsel's objection — that this strips the member of hearing rights this bill exists to confer — is not answered by the Director's direction; it is overridden by it**, and that is the Director's call to make. What remains is M 2's own floor: a 30-day window, written reasons, a 90-day duty to act, and tolling. **A second consequence is new and is carded at item 124:** Article 3 contains § 2.2-4021, so with the limb there is now **no** deemed decision on a reconsideration the Board sits on — by express exclusion rather than by argument — and a member whose reconsideration is never completed has no case decision and so no Article 5 route. Item 124 drafts the cure and does not enact it. **Options (a), (b) and (c) are closed.** **No figure moves.**

89. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 49, from [MOO-120](/MOO/issues/MOO-120)'s pass 12, finding F-1 — FATAL, and the only item on this list that stops the bill working.** G 4's settlement bar forbids settlement both after a determination has taken effect **and** after *"the reservation for the election has been released in accordance with subdivision D 7."* Item 54 disapplies the first limb only, and G 3 *commands* the D 7 release — so item 54 is inoperative in every case it was written for, and the survivor gets the crediting and no subsidy. **Two insertions cure it; they are drafted in [`moo120-pass12-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-moo120-pass12-disposition) §7 and enacted nowhere.** Insertion (i) sits in the rescission sentence, which already covers *"a determination under subdivision 2 or 3"*, so **it cures the pre-existing G 2 branch at the same time — a correction to my own earlier statement that the G 2 branch needed separate treatment.** Recommend the complete cure.

**CLOSED in store revision 52. The Director answered *cure in full*, and both insertions are enacted** — the D 7 release limb disapplied in the rescission sentence, which reaches the pre-existing G 2 branch as well as G 3's, and the reinstatement limb extended to a reservation released in accordance with D 7 where the determination is later rescinded, reversed or modified. **The §7 item 40 safeguards on the over-cap reinstatement route apply to the enlarged trigger unchanged.** §5.31, §6.0-52 items 1 and 5.

90. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 49, from pass 12's S-1 — serious. Subdivision J 1's terminal date can never arrive, on two independent grounds, and one of them is item 49(iii)'s own new words.** Limb (a) requires every accepted election to have been settled, cancelled under G 5 or determined, and **G 2's fourth sentence excepts two classes from any determination at all** (cancelled under D 6; reservation released under D 7 with no credit given) — one such election and limb (a) is false forever. Limb (b), *"the date of the last rescission under subdivision G 4"*, is **not ascertainable prospectively and has no outer bound**, because the death limb stays live for a parker until she dies. Both repairs drafted; recommend both, with limb (b) replaced by one further report after a late rescission.

**CLOSED in store revision 52. The Director answered *both repairs*, and both are enacted** — limb (a) excepts cancellation under D 6 and a D 7 release with no credit given; limb (b) is bounded to rescissions already occurred; and a later rescission draws one further report, limited to that rescission and its consequences, by the first December 1 after it. **The reporting duty can now end.** §6.0-52 items 6 and 7.

91. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 49, from pass 12's S-2 with M-6 — serious, and the severity is higher than either the reviewer or I first put it.** G 4 clause (ii) says subdivisions 2 and 3 *"shall not apply"* to a member approved for disability, with no filing-date condition; item 49(i) denies the rescission where the application was filed after the determination took effect. **The text therefore says both that subdivision 2 never applied to her and that its determination stands.** And § 51.1-156 H's **second** sentence **requires** the Board to waive the ninety-day period where the Medical Board certifies she had no knowledge of her disability and could not with reasonable inquiry have found it — so the bill removes the remedy in the one class the Code **compels** the Board to admit. Candidate text: a carve-out bounded to H's mandatory limb, plus an express saving for every other late filing. **Recommend both.**

**CLOSED in store revision 52. The Director answered *the carve-out and the saving together*, and both are enacted**, with the carve-out bounded to § 51.1-156 H's mandatory limb in H's own words — verified verbatim against the section on the day of the write. §5.31, §6.0-52 items 8 and 9.

92. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 49, from pass 12's S-3 — serious.** G 4 clause (iii), **error of the Board**, has no rescission route at all: the rescission sentence runs on disability approval and on death only. So where the Board concedes its own error after the determination has taken effect, clause (iii) says on its face that subdivision 2 *"shall not apply"*, the credit is reduced anyway, and the settlement bar blocks settlement. **This is the one of the three clause-events where the equities run hardest against the Board and it is the only one the nine edits left out.** Candidate text drafted; recommend enacting. Subdivision B 4 f's carve-out is untouched.

**CLOSED in store revision 52. The Director answered *extend*, and it is enacted** in clause (iii)'s own words, with 60 days running from the Board's own determination and the redetermination clock conformed to the new trigger. **Subdivision B 4 f's carve-out is untouched.** §6.0-52 items 2 and 10.

93. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 49, from pass 12's S-4 — serious as reported, confirmed in part, and the mechanism as stated does not operate.** The reviewer's engine is that G 2's 120-day bound forces the determination to take effect while the Board still has 90 days on reconsideration. **It does not: the tolling the Director enacted as item 28 stops *"a period within which a determination is required to take effect"* while a subsection M request is pending**, and a reconsideration request is such a request. What survives is the member who asks **after** effect, or whose determination takes effect during the pendency anyway: she wins and **no provision restores her credit, deadlines the restoration, or pays her the difference.** Candidate text drafted; recommend enacting. **ANNOTATED IN STORE REVISION 50: a second reviewer has reached this gap from a different direction, which is the strongest thing that can be said for enacting it.** Pass 16's R-3 found it by attacking the clause store revision 47 lifted out of subsection M 2 — on its Reading B the Board's clock resumes while her reconsideration is unanswered, and if it then succeeds **nothing in subdivision G 4 lets the Board rescind**. **I have not drafted a second version of this repair**, because two divergent repairs for one defect would be worse than one. **I recommend enacting yours.**

**CLOSED in store revision 52. The Director answered *enact the restoration-and-arrears duty*, and it is enacted** — restore or credit, recertify and settle, 60 days, and arrears in the next payment. **Two repairs were required to make it operative and both are mine**: the settlement bar and the released reservation had to be disapplied or the sentence commanded a settlement two others forbade, and the arrears formula had to reach a *modification* as well as a reversal. §6.0-52 items 1, 4 and 11. **The arrears formula is carded as item 105, and pass 17's S-3 moves its residual defect to item 116.**

94. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 49, from pass 12's M-2 — one word, and it is carded rather than enacted for a reason I want recorded.** The item-48 confining sentence operates *"[f]or the purposes of this definition"*; its twin, *"qualifying retirement"*, carries the same § 51.1-155.1 gateway unconfined. On my reading the confinement has nothing to bite on in an actual-facts test — **but that reading is exactly what a reader will argue against by *expressio unius*, and it is the ground I gave the Director for the asymmetry.** *"For the purposes of this section"* closes it for one word. Recommend enacting; reversing my own stated ground is not a drafting repair.

**CLOSED in store revision 52. The Director checked it, and the one word is enacted** — the confinement operates *"for the purposes of this section."* The asymmetry with the untouched twin is removed without amending the twin, and all 12 uses of the definition were chased: the direction is exclusion or no effect in every one. §5.31.

95. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 49, from pass 12's M-5 — moderate, and item 46 is what creates it.** § 51.1-156 D allows a disability effective date up to 90 days before the notice is filed, so a member paid a service allowance during pendency and then approved retroactively has been **paid twice for the same months** — and G 4's no-collection limb bars the Board from offsetting it, because the approval is *"an event to which this subdivision applies."* Pass 12 puts it at **$1,300 to $2,500 per affected member** on a headcount well under 0.1 expected, falling on the **trust fund**, reimbursed by nothing, and appearing in no line of J 1. Two candidate texts; **recommend the narrow one that authorises the single offset rather than the one that takes a protection from every member.**

**CLOSED in store revision 52. The Director checked it, and limb (b) is enacted** — the one month-for-month offset is authorised and nothing else is. **G 4's no-collection limb is untouched**, so no protection is taken from any member to reach it. §6.0-52 item 12.

96. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 49. Mine, carried from the store revision 42 card unanswered.** G 3's second sentence provides that a member credited under G 3 *"shall not be treated as having reached his unreduced retirement eligibility date by reason of the election."* Item 54 now rescinds that determination and settles her subsidy, while that sentence still withholds the fact the *"certified subsidy"* definition and the subsection C clause (ii) cap both read. **Pass 12 says the defect is a genuine ambiguity rather than a certainty** — on rescission she is credited afresh in G 4 — **and that it does not reach the floor or the cap by the route I asked about.** Seventeen words drafted; recommend enacting, because every member it reaches has already been approved for disability or has died.

**CLOSED in store revision 52. The Director checked it, and the sentence is enacted** — G 3's second sentence does not apply where a G 3 determination is rescinded under subdivision 4. §6.0-52 item 13.

97. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 49. Mine, and it is the half of the parity the item-54 answer asked for that I did not enact.** Store revision 49 imports the death branch's **recertification** limb into item 54's valuation sentence; the death branch also **refunds the excess** of amounts paid over the cost of the credit credited, and that limb is not imported. So a member who paid for 9.4 months and is credited with 9 keeps nothing back for the part-month. **It is a payment out that no answer authorised**, which is why it is drafted and not enacted. Recommend enacting.

**CLOSED in store revision 52. The Director checked it, and the refund limb is enacted**, with the death branch's own 60-day payment deadline and its adverse-tax fallback, because the carded limb created a payment duty with no date. **That addition is mine** — §6.0-52 item 3.

98. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 49. Carried from the store revision 42 card unanswered.** Should G 4's bar on determining while a disability application is pending, and its tolling, follow the item-54 rescission to **subdivision 3**? **Recommend leaving it**: the bar stops the Board making a subdivision 2 determination at a time of its choosing, and a subdivision 3 crediting follows non-completion by the completion date rather than a Board decision.

**STILL OPEN. Not asked on the store revision 49 card and not enacted.** My recommendation is unchanged: leave it.

Carried from the store revision 42 card unanswered.** Should the § 30-19.1:7 statement **quantify** the two one-sided costs the Director knowingly bought — the pendency allowance under item 46 that subsection F's no-collection limb makes irrecoverable, and item 50's irrecoverable clause (ii) refund? **Recommend yes.** Neither answer is reopened; the only question is whether the fiscal statement names the amounts rather than leaving them to be found. The amounts are [@Fiscal & Impact Modeler](agent://428b53bf-f49e-4505-a607-3560a26b3d50)'s, not mine.

The Director checked it.** §3 now requires the § 30-19.1:7 statement to quantify both one-sided costs, and the amounts are requested from [@Fiscal & Impact Modeler](agent://428b53bf-f49e-4505-a607-3560a26b3d50) rather than asserted here. **Neither of the Director's underlying answers — item 46 and item 50 — is reopened.**

100. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 50, from [MOO-135](/MOO/issues/MOO-135)'s pass 16, finding R-4's second half, and it belongs with item 73.** Where the Board **never gives** the subdivision G 2 notice, store revision 50 removes the irreconcilable commands but the determination still cannot take effect, and subdivision G 2 forbids payment of **any** retirement allowance until it does — so **her allowance may be withheld with no outer bound, lawfully on the face of the bill**, because subsection F provides in terms that the deferral is not a collection, offset or withholding. In the **late**-notice case the position is coherent and still unbounded: pass 16's example is a notice 400 days late and an allowance withheld about 430 days, where before store revision 47's E5 the outer limit was 120 days — unsatisfiable in that case, but a bound. And when the determination finally takes effect her first payment carries *"every amount that would have been payable"* — **the arrears, and no provision of this section provides interest on them**, so the Board's lateness is an interest-free deferral at her expense. Pass 16's honest statement of it: *"the Board gains no money by being late, but it gains time, and the member funds it."* **Three options, each drafted in full at [`pass16-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-pass16-disposition) §7, and none enacted.** **(a)** A backstop effective date: the determination takes effect on a stated day after the determination date whether or not notice has been given, which cures the withholding and takes away the notice-before-effect protection pass 4 required. **(b)** Pay the allowance, subject to later adjustment, where the determination has not taken effect within a stated period — pass 16's own suggestion, and my recommendation, because it keeps her protection and ends the withholding; its cost is that the trust pays an allowance that may later be reduced, and the adjustment machinery is three sentences. **(c)** Statutory interest on the arrears at the rate subdivision B 4 a already defines, which answers the funding point and not the withholding. **This is a money provision and it is yours.** What store revision 50 enacted is the removal of the impossible command and a subsection J report line for a notice never given; it does not bound the withholding and I am not representing that it does.

101. **NEW — [MOO-149](/MOO/issues/MOO-149), record-only. No bill text, no figure, nothing renumbered.** Five §0 designators the bill cites, and what each is cited for: `§0.00000000000` the § 51.1-155 sweep; `§0.00000000000a` the dropped-direction process note; `§0.0000000000b` the crediting-date cure; `§0.000000000000000` *"alone would have been a defect"*; `§0.000000000000000000` a bare trailing citation. [`moo101-cut-record`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-moo101-cut-record) §13 reported all five stranded; **re-swept at store revision 50, only the two lettered ones are, the other three resolving to a same-designator banner in the retained record.** A record repair, not an amendment; no operative cross-reference is touched.

102. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 51, and it is mine rather than a reviewer's. The 524,288-character ceiling is
    now the binding constraint on this document, and the next pass will not fit.** This revision had
    **886 characters** of headroom and needed 6,649, so it paid for itself by moving §7 item 22 — answered,
    nothing open — verbatim to the retained-record document. That bought headroom back to about **6,800
    characters**, which is one more pass of this size and no more.

    **Why it is yours and not mine.** The cheap moves are spent. §7.1, every §0 record for revisions 2
    through 49, and every §6.0-n record are already pointers; §7 is **263,611 characters**, half the file,
    and §1 — the operative bill — is only 101,063 of it. Continuing one answered item at a time means the
    record of *why* each choice was made drifts across three documents and the pointers start pointing at
    pointers. **No option here touches operative text**; this is about where the record lives and how a
    reader finds it.

    **Three ways to go, and I recommend (b).** **(a)** Carry on item by item, moving the next answered §7
    item each revision — cheapest now, worst later, and it is the status quo. **(b)** Move the **answered**
    §7 items as one block to a dedicated *closed-items* document, keeping §7 for live questions only:
    items 18, 22, 27, 28, 29, 30, 34, 35 and 60 are about **48,000 characters** between them, which is
    roughly seven passes of headroom, and §7 would then read as what its heading says it is. **(c)** Split
    the bill text itself from the notes — `bill-text-s8` keeping §1 and §2, a companion taking §3 to §8 —
    which is the largest change and the only one that re-points every internal reference in the file.

    **What I need from you is only the choice.** I will do the move and the cross-reference check either
    way, and any of the three is reversible because nothing is summarised away. **I have not acted on (b)
    or (c)**; this revision did (a) once, because the corrections it carries had to land.

103. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 52. Mine, created by the Director's item-92 answer.** Item 92 gives the Board's determination of **its own error** a rescission trigger, and G 4 already owes that determination within 60 days of the member's final qualifying retirement date. But **after a subdivision 2 determination has taken effect there is no route by which the member can ask for it** — she can seek review of the determination under subsection M, which tests the determination rather than the error, and the error determination itself is the Board's to initiate. So the one clause-event where the Board concedes its own fault is the one that depends on the Board noticing. **Should the member be able to file written evidence of an error of the Board and oblige a determination, as she already may for an involuntary separation?** G 4's involuntary-separation sentence is the model and is four lines long: she files, the Board determines within 30 days, gives written notice with reasons, and the determination is reviewable under subsection M. **Recommend enacting it** — it costs the Board a duty it already owes on its own motion and gives the member the only route to a remedy the Director just created. **Drafted in full** in [`moo64-rev52-record`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-moo64-rev52-record) §4, and **enacted nowhere**. **PASS 17 UPGRADES THE GROUND AND THE RECOMMENDATION: necessary, not optional.** Its S-7 shows the clause (iii) trigger is **unenforceable** without this item — after a subdivision 2 determination takes effect there is no period within which the Board must determine its own error, no route by which the member may ask, and no notice duty; subsection M 2 reaches a failure to determine only *"within a period required by this section,"* and no period is required for this one. **The cheapest path to nominal compliance is silence, and silence is complete.** **Pass 17's M-9 is held to this item:** the redetermination clock's new limb has no fallback where the Board never determines, and that fallback cannot be drafted until this item decides what period the Board is under. **ANSWERED AND CLOSED IN STORE REVISION 60: ENACT, confined to the period after a determination under subdivision 2 or 3 has taken effect, so that one determination does not acquire two clocks (§6.0-60 item 8, answering pass 18's M18-9, which showed this item's stated ground was too broad — G 4 already supplies a period, a notice duty and a review route for the clause (iii) determination, and what is missing is the LATER determination in the rescission sentence). **PASS 17's M-9 IS ALSO CLOSED, WITH NO TEXT:** the redetermination clock needs no fallback, because its sentence opens *"Where the Board rescinds a determination"* and the rescission cannot occur without the determination; what the member needed was a remedy for the absent determination, and this item gives her one (§6.0-60 item 3). This item is not an open question.**

104. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 52.
