Mine, and it is a reporting gap rather than a member's cheque.** Subdivision J 1 states *"the number of determinations rescinded and the service credit restored under subdivision G 4."* After this revision there are two things it does not reach: the **refunds** item 97 now requires on a subdivision-3 rescission, which are a payment out of the trust that appears in no line of J 1; and the **reversals and modifications** item 93 now acts on, which are not rescissions and so are counted nowhere. **Should J 1 state both?** One clause does it, and it is the **companion-record** version rather than the one this item first carried — pass 17's m-3 found two texts under one item number and the record's is the better: *"; the number of determinations reversed or modified on reconsideration or on judicial review and the service credit restored or credited on account of them; and every amount paid or refunded under subdivision G 4 on account of a rescission, reversal, or modification."* It catches the **restored service credit** and the word ***"refunded,"*** which is what the refund limb actually does, and the version this item carried through store revision 55 missed both. **Recommend enacting.** The money is small and the point is that the Governor and the money committees can see it at all — and item 99's quantification will be read against these lines. **Drafted here and in [`moo64-rev52-record`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-moo64-rev52-record) §4 in identical words, enacted nowhere.** **ANSWERED AND CLOSED IN STORE REVISION 60: ENACT, in the companion-record wording. J 1 now reports the determinations reversed or modified on reconsideration or on judicial review and the service credit restored or credited on account of them, and every amount paid or refunded under subdivision G 4 on account of a rescission, reversal, or modification. This item is not an open question.**

105. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 52. Mine, and it is the one of my four drafting changes that moves money, which is why it is here as well as in the note.** Item 93's arrears limb as carded paid the member *"every amount that would have been payable … had the determination **not taken effect**."* On a **reversal** that is right. On a **modification** — a smaller reduction left standing on reconsideration or by a court — it pays her as though no determination had ever been made, which **overpays her against the determination that survives**, and subsection F's no-collection limb means the Commonwealth never recovers it. **I enacted *"had the determination, as reversed or modified, taken effect on the date on which the determination took effect"***, which is word-for-word identical in effect on a reversal and is the only non-absurd reading on a modification. **Recommend it stand.** If the Director would rather the member be paid as though no determination existed in both cases, that is a policy choice and one sentence reverses it — **but it should be a choice and not a side effect of my wording.**

**CLOSED in store revision 57 by [MOO-152](/MOO/issues/MOO-152)'s pass 17, which worked the modification-in-her-favour case and confirms the enacted formula is right and does not underpay her. The enacted form stands. But the same sentence has a larger defect neither this item nor its carding found: the formula is GROSS, NOT NET — it was lifted from subdivision 2's arrears sentence, which is safe only because subdivision 2 forbids any payment before its determination takes effect. On the review's facts, $8,280 owed against $64,080 commanded. That residual is §7 item 116, and the bar on recovering the excess is G 4's own no-collection sentence rather than subsection F's, whose limb is tied to the failure to retire.**

106. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 54. Four things your six answers produced, and the first is the only one
    where the enacted words are not your words.** Nothing here is drafted into the bill. Items 1 and 2
    are decisions; items 3 and 4 are named risks with no drafted cure, because a cure would be a new
    policy.

    **1. The pre-filing disclosure says something different from what you checked, and you should either
    confirm it or send it back.** You approved: *"the benefit payable on the member's death is determined
    under subsection C of § 51.1-165."* **(f), which you approved on the same card, makes that false for
    exactly the members who receive the writing** — a member whose election the Board has accepted is
    inside clause (i) of G 4, so § 51.1-163 does not apply and § 51.1-162 governs. I enacted the duty with
    the content the act now produces. **Alternative A restores your words verbatim**, in one edit, and the
    cost of taking it is that the Board must tell every member something the act contradicts four
    subdivisions later. **Alternative B** is narrower than either: state the § 51.1-165 C rule **and** the
    (f) exception, so a member who later cancels her election under subdivision G 5 — and who is then
    outside G 4 entirely — is told the rule that will actually govern her. **B is the only version that is
    true for both populations the writing reaches**, it is about 40 words longer, and I have not drafted
    it in because choosing it is yours. §6.0-54 item 3.

    **2. Answer 7 releases the cap and does not recover the cash. Is that what you meant?** Subsection N
    compels the Commonwealth to pay the settled amount to the retirement system. The enacted sentence
    restores the amount to what is **available under the limitation in the first sentence of subdivision
    D 2** — so one further election's worth of cap comes back, which at the central $94,649 is the figure
    [MOO-105](/MOO/issues/MOO-105) priced. **The money already paid stays paid.** Recovering it would take
    it back out of the trust fund after settlement, which is a different proposition and one that
    [MOO-96](/MOO/issues/MOO-96)'s **F-3** and Va. Const. art. X, § 11 both bear on. Say so if you want it
    drafted; I have not.

    **3. (a) was not selected, and the class it reaches is now settled against rather than arguable.** A
    member who elected an option under § 51.1-165 naming a sibling, an adult child or an unmarried
    partner is reached by no limb of § 51.1-162 and not by § 51.1-165 C 2. (f) routes her death to
    § 51.1-162 and § 51.1-162 B pays only a surviving spouse, minor child or parent. **She paid for the
    credit, made the election, and her contingent annuitant receives nothing from either.** (a) is 236
    characters and remains drafted at item 60. **This is a decision the card made, and it should appear in
    the memo's risk section as one.**

    **4. The subsection J hook is drafted at the death notice only.** You selected the three disclosure and
    reporting repairs as a set, and the set's third limb was *the subsection J reporting hook for a death
    notice the Board fails to give*. That is what is enacted. G 4's three other notice duties — the
    rescission notice, the clause (iii) determination notice and the involuntary-separation notice — have
    no such hook, and the clause (iii) one already has a report line of its own. **A general hook for
    every notice in the subdivision is one sentence and it is not drafted**, because it is wider than what
    you approved.

107. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 55, from [MOO-131](/MOO/issues/MOO-131) — the four residues of this cut, drafted and enacted nowhere.** Each is drafted in full at [`moo131-conforming-record`](/MOO/issues/MOO-131#document-moo131-conforming-record) §5 so it can be enacted with one substitution. **(a) E1's new deadline makes D 2's notice performable with zero margin, and 30 days may be the wrong period.** Both duties now fall on the thirtieth day after completion, so the Board complies only by determining early — and pass 12's own failure scenario treats **day 58** as ordinary actuarial practice. This is MOO-130's **M6** in D 2's clothing; the commission records M6 as needing no bill edit, so nothing is enacted, and **I am not resolving by drafting choice a question about how long an actuarial determination takes.** The alternative, if you want margin, reads the notice *"no later than 30 days after the Board determines the certified subsidy for the election, and in any event no later than 60 days after the applicant's application is completed"* — one substitution, and taking it would **not** require reversing E1, which independently closes D 3 and D 7. **(b) E5's deeming confers the right, and M 2's 30-day window is keyed to a notice several deemed actions have no duty to give** — D 7's expiration of a reservation, G 1's post-retirement redetermination and subsection E's crediting date carry no notice duty at all, and four further notice duties state no period, which the failure limb does not reach. The likely reading is that the bar never begins and so never bites, which favours the member; the contrary reading is that she has no window and therefore no route. **The right is conferred either way; the time limit on it is what is uncertain**, and one clause appended to M 2's first sentence would settle it. **(c) A withdrawal under E8 stops nothing, and E1 is what makes that bite.** Before this cut no Board duty ran from completion to the certified subsidy, so an application withdrawn on day 5 left nothing outstanding; E1 creates one, so the Board now owes a mandatory determination within 30 days on an application nobody is pursuing, and E7 makes the omission reportable. **Enactment 3 clause (v) already assumes a withdrawal discharges the saved duties** — *"until discharged, the application is withdrawn, or the applicant dies"* — while nothing says so for the section in effect. One phrase closes it, and *"discharged"* is clause (v)'s own word. **(d) E5's deeming widens what can enter M 2's reconsideration, and this must be decided together with §7 item 88.** Item 88 records why that review sentence was twice withdrawn: **§ 2.2-4021 B supplies a deemed decision in the named party's favour** where an agency does not decide within 90 days in an Article 3 proceeding and is then served notice; VRS is not among the three exceptions and § 2.2-4018's exemptions do not reach retirement determinations. **E5 is not that sentence**, and the distinction is the whole of my answer: it deems **acts and events, never inaction** — every noun in the list is something that happens — and it deems them *"for the purposes of this subsection,"* not *"a case decision for the purposes of the Administrative Process Act,"* which was the operative language of the withdrawn string. What it **does** do is widen the matters that can enter M 2's reconsideration, from the fourteen expressly named plus the general gateway to thirteen classes of act across the section, so **if M 2's written reconsideration were ever held to be an Article 3 informal fact-finding, the § 2.2-4021 B surface widens with it** — a question about **M 2's character**, which exists today and which E5 enlarges rather than creates. **The narrower alternative you named in the commission — two sentences in D 5, both lifted from D 3 — is drafted at the record's §5 (d) and is not enacted**; it closes the funding-queue denial, which is the one decision that actually denies a member the subsidy, and leaves M1's asymmetry open. **Item 88 and this item should be decided together, because the answer to item 88 may change which of the two you want.** This is the one I would put in front of counsel first.

108. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 56, from [MOO-153](/MOO/issues/MOO-153)'s counsel pass, finding S-2 — serious, and the worst thing on this list that is not already fatal. The Board can meet every one of the roughly seventy periods in § 51.1-142.4 and still leave an eligible member's application permanently unable to advance, with no reconsideration gateway and no Article 5 route.** **No provision obliges the Board to (i) file the subdivision D 3 request for the Director of Planning and Budget's written confirmation or (ii) issue the certification required by subsection D.** D 1's bar is purely negative; D 3's first sentence is a condition precedent with no duty to seek; D 3's 15-day clock runs *"after the confirmation is obtained"* and so never starts; D 3's 90-day notice fires only where a request *"has been pending."* **Re-keyed against the head counsel could not see:** store revision 55's E1 clocked D 1's certified-subsidy determination at 30 days, so **exactly two clocks are missing** and the stranding survives. Worked case: the member with 29 years 11 months who files 1 July 2029, holds a compliant **favourable** determination by 31 July 2029, and on 1 July 2030 has no certification, no reservation, no acceptance and no notice — M 2 fails on both limbs, because the determination favoured her and every period was met. **Edit 1 does not reach it; it moves the stall one step downstream.** Distinct from item 24 (DPB never answers a request that *was* filed) and item 75. **Options, drafted verbatim at [`moo153-counsel-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-145#document-moo153-counsel-disposition) §4:** **(a)** nothing, and I recommend against it; **(b)** **two clocks** — file the D 3 request within 15 days of completion, give the certification within 15 days of the confirmation; **(c)** the request clock alone, which makes item 24 visible at 105 days but leaves the certification unclocked; **(d)** a single 120-day outer limit, one sentence, but it buries DPB's own 30 days inside a deadline the Board cannot always meet. **I recommend (b)**: it converts the stall into a period failure **M 2 already reaches**, which is far smaller than adding review rights. **No figure moves**, though each stalled application parks up to $204,000 against the $3,000,000 — a headroom question for Fiscal & Impact Modeler if the Director wants it sized. **The Director's: it creates a duty to ask that the agreed design does not contain, and sets two numbers.**

    **ANSWERED AND ENACTED IN STORE REVISION 59** — the Policy Director's direction of 12 October 2026, 13:26 UTC. **Option (b), both clocks.** Subdivision D 3 takes a new **second** sentence — *"The Board shall file the request required by this subdivision no later than 15 days after the member's application is completed."* Subdivision D 1 takes a new **third** sentence — *"The Board shall give the certification required by this subsection no later than 15 days after the confirmation required by subdivision 3 is obtained."* Both as drafted at [`moo153-counsel-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-145#document-moo153-counsel-disposition) §4, word for word. **The *"or else"* is borrowed and not written:** each is now *"a failure … to make a determination or to give a notice within a period required by this section"* for subdivision M 2, and a reportable failure for subsection C's reporting sentence and for subdivision J 1. **One near-duplication examined and left alone:** subdivision D 3's fifth sentence already requires the Board to *"act upon the application no later than 15 days after the confirmation is obtained."* The new D 1 sentence is the same period from the same trigger but on a different act — *giving the certification* rather than *acting upon the application* — so the two are consistent and neither is surplus; collapsing them would have required choosing which act the single clock governs, and that is a policy choice. Recorded at §6.0-59.

109. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 56, from counsel's M-1 — moderate as reported, narrower after re-keying.** M 2's gateway and subsection C's reporting sentence reach only a failure *"to make a determination or to give a notice,"* and **stating a matter in a certification is neither** — so an omitted statement in the certification D 1 makes the condition of acceptance looks unreviewable and unreportable. **Re-keyed: M 2's second sentence already deems a *"certification"* a determination for that subsection's purposes**, so a certification that issues and omits a required statement **is already reviewable**. The residue is (i) subsection C's reporting sentence, which genuinely does not reach a failure to state, and (ii) the certification that never issues, which is item 108's clock and not a words problem. **Options:** **(a)** nothing; **(b)** add *"or to state a matter this section requires it to state"* to subsection C's reporting sentence only; **(c)** counsel's form, which adds it to M 2's first sentence too. **I recommend (b), and only if item 108 is answered** — a duty to state has no period of its own until the certification has one. The Director's, because (b) widens a reporting duty.

    **ANSWERED AND ENACTED IN STORE REVISION 59** — the Policy Director's direction of 12 October 2026, 13:26 UTC. **Option (b), subsection C only, not counsel's wider form.** The reporting sentence now reads *"every failure to make a determination, **to state a matter this subsection requires it to state,** or to give a notice, required by this subsection within the period this subsection allows for it."* Not added to M 2, for the reason given above and unchanged: M 2's second sentence already deems a *certification* a determination, so a certification that issues and omits a required statement is already reviewable. **The ordinal in this item and in item 108 was wrong and is corrected above:** the reporting sentence is subsection C's **eighth**, not its seventh — the sixth and seventh are the two boundary sentences item 111 proposed to delete. The amended words are unaffected by the error; only the label was. **Subsection C still has nine sentences.**

110. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 56, from counsel's S-3 — serious, and it reaches a decision already taken.** § 2.2-4021 B provides that where an agency does not decide within 90 days of an informal fact-finding and the named party serves written notice and 30 more days pass, *"the decision shall be deemed to be in favor of the named party"*; the only carve-outs are three environmental ones. Worked case: a member refused by the $170,000 ceiling requests M 2 reconsideration on 4 February 2030, the Board holds an informal fact-finding on 4 March 2030 and sits, and on 2 July 2030 **general law deems the decision in her favour** — against a ceiling this record calls self-executing, against D 1's bar, against D 3's condition precedent and against the $3,000,000. **The exposure is already live without option (d):** VRS is **not** exempt under § 2.2-4002 and § 2.2-4001 clause (ii) reaches these determinations on its face, so **no option (a) to (d) of item 88 addresses § 2.2-4021 B.** (d) does not create it; it converts a strong argument into a certainty. **The instrument is not the optional second limb disapplying Articles 3 and 4** — that strips the member of the hearing rights this bill exists to confer — **but a carve-out from the deemed-decision sentence alone**, drafted verbatim in the companion document at §4: *the sentence of subsection B of § 2.2-4021 providing that a decision shall be deemed to be in favor of the named party, and the corresponding sentence of subsection C, shall not apply to a determination of the Board under this section.* That the General Assembly wrote three such exceptions into that very sentence **is** the authority for the form. **Placement:** **(a)** a new subdivision M 3 of § 51.1-142.4 — minimal footprint, no amendment to § 2.2-4021 and no sweep across every agency, and **I recommend it**; **(b)** amend § 2.2-4021 B itself, opening a general-law section for one program; **(c)** an enactment clause, which I recommend against because the rule is permanent. **I would want this clause with whichever of item 88's options is taken, and even if item 88 is answered by doing nothing.** The Director's, with item 88.

    **ANSWERED AND ENACTED IN STORE REVISION 59** — the Policy Director's direction of 12 October 2026, 13:26 UTC. **Option (a), a new subdivision M 3 of § 51.1-142.4**, verbatim as drafted: *"The sentence of subsection B of § 2.2-4021 providing that a decision shall be deemed to be in favor of the named party, and the corresponding sentence of subsection C of that section, shall not apply to a determination of the Board under this section or to a reconsideration under subdivision 2."* **No amendment to § 2.2-4021 and no conforming sweep across the agencies that section reaches.** § 2.2-4021 verified at source on 12 October 2026: it sits in **Article 3 (Case Decisions)** of Chapter 40 of Title 2.2, subsection B carries the 90-day/30-day deemed-decision default and subsection C the corresponding 30-day one after a hearing officer's recommendation, and the General Assembly wrote its own environmental exceptions into both — which is the authority for this form. **One consequence of answering this item and item 88 together is new and is carded at item 123:** because § 2.2-4021 is in Article 3, item 88's optional limb already disapplies it to a *reconsideration*, so M 3's reconsideration limb is now belt-and-braces. It is kept, and the reason is at item 123.

111. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 56, from counsel's M-2 — moderate, and it reverses two drafting choices the Director's own directions produced.** Subsection C's third sentence bars acceptance where *"the amount determined under clause (ii) of this subsection is zero."* For the empty-solution-set class the fifth sentence directs the Board, *"in place of determining and stating an amount under that clause,"* to determine that no amount is specified — so **there is no amount determined under clause (ii) at all**, and the bar fails for absence of a referent rather than being satisfied. Sentences 6 and 7 — pass 15's boundary and pass 16's world-side boundary, the latter directed by the Director and accepted on [MOO-145](/MOO/issues/MOO-145) — then deny that that amount is zero, **which asserts it exists in order to deny its value**, and hand VRS an *expressio unius* argument. Counsel also answers the question those sentences were written for: **they do not stop the analyst who enters `0`, and no sentence in the Code can.** That is an M 1 procedures matter, and the member's protection is C's fourth-sentence notice plus M 2 within 30 days. **Options:** **(a)** keep both; **(b)** amend the bar to read *"where the Board has determined an amount under clause (ii) of this subsection and that amount is zero"* and **delete both boundary sentences**, taking subsection C from nine sentences to **seven** — counsel's sweep and mine agree that **no sentence-ordinal citation in §1 points into subsection C**, so nothing renumbers. **I recommend (b)**: it reads on an actual determination and on nothing else, and it is a net reduction. **The Director's, because (a) is what two of their own directions produced.** No figure moves on either.

    **ANSWERED AND NOT ENACTED IN STORE REVISION 59** — the Policy Director's direction of 12 October 2026, 13:26 UTC. **Do not enact.** The zero bar stays on the record side as *"the amount determined under clause (ii) of this subsection is zero"*, and **both boundary sentences stay** — subsection C's sixth (the characterisation boundary, store revision 48's edit 2) and its seventh (the world-side boundary, pass 16's M-3). **Subsection C remains at nine sentences** and no designator moves. Counsel's M-2 therefore stands unanswered on the record as a known, accepted cost: an analyst who meets an empty clause (ii) field and enters `0` makes an entry the seventh sentence says is not a determination of zero, so the bar is not engaged — which is the answer M-2 wanted — but the bar's own words still speak to the **entry** rather than to the **determination**, and a reader has to carry two sentences to get there. **I am not reopening it.** The declined text remains drafted verbatim at [`moo153-counsel-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-145#document-moo153-counsel-disposition) §4 if the Director ever wants it.

112. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 56, from counsel's M-4 — the publication posture of this file, and the Director's alone.** This document is **513,144 characters**; §1, the bill text proper with its enactment clauses, is about **108,000**. The rest is commentary, §7 items, sweeps and pass dispositions carrying sentences such as *"it does not cure the stranding; it documents it"* and *"FATAL, and the only item on this list that stops the bill working."* **The §7 item list is an admission against interest and it travels in the same file as the bill** — counsel wrote the opposition's floor speech out of this record alone, at §1 of its report. **Minimum ask: the bill text and the §7 item list should be separate documents before anything leaves Paperclip.** Nothing here is cleared for external use and nothing has left Paperclip. No privilege question is raised; the point is that these are one file.

    **ANSWERED AND ENACTED IN STORE REVISION 59** — the Policy Director's direction of 12 October 2026, 13:26 UTC. **Split performed in store revision 59.** §7's whole item list — every numbered item 1 to 125, the §7.1 retained-record stub, and the preamble — is moved **verbatim** to a new document, [`bill-text-s8-item-list`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-bill-text-s8-item-list), and §7 of the bill is now a pointer stub. A reference of the form **§7 item n** still resolves, in that document. **The bill document falls from 510,963 characters to 249,041, of which §1 is 112,886** — the first time since store revision 33 that it is not within a few thousand characters of the store's 524,288-character ceiling, and the end of the headroom problem that forced seven record moves. **Nothing in the item list is deleted and nothing is reworded by the move**; the two ordinal corrections and the seven dispositions in this revision are marked as such where they occur. **One housekeeping repair taken with the move:** items 114 to 121 had been written *after* the §7.1 retained-record heading in store revision 57, so eight live items sat inside a stub that says its content was moved out. The §7.1 heading and its three paragraphs are moved to the end of the item list, after item 125, and **the numbered items are restored to numeric order** — 1 to 125, where the file previously read 1 to 113, then 122, then 114 to 121. **No item's text is altered by the reordering and the reassembled file is the same length to the character**, which is how it was verified.

113. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 56, from counsel's m-2 — minor, attributable to store revision 52 and not to any edit of mine, and flagged rather than pressed because [MOO-152](/MOO/issues/MOO-152) owns that revision.** Subdivision J 1's third sentence limits the post-terminal further report to *"that rescission and its consequences,"* but **ten provisions outside J 1** require the Board to state matters in the subsection J report, and a matter arising after the last annual report has nowhere to go. **Counsel's one-clause fix is incomplete, and I say so:** extending the further report's **content** does not help, because its **trigger** is a rescission under subdivision G 4 — absent a rescission there is no further report at all. **Options:** **(a)** extend the content only; **(b)** give the further report an independent trigger — *where any matter this section requires the Board to state arises after the last report required by this subdivision, the Board shall make one further report of that matter no later than the first December 1 following it* — which cures both, and **which I recommend**. Both drafted in the companion document at §4. Routed to the Director for direction and to [MOO-152](/MOO/issues/MOO-152) for notice; I have not touched J 1.

    **ANSWERED AND ENACTED IN STORE REVISION 59** — the Policy Director's direction of 12 October 2026, 13:26 UTC. **Option (b), the independent trigger.** Subdivision J 1's third sentence now reads *"Where a rescission under subdivision G 4 occurs after the Board has made the last report required by this subdivision, **or where any matter this section requires the Board to state in the report required by this subdivision arises after the Board has made that report,** the Board shall make one further report, limited to that rescission **or matter** and its consequences, no later than the first December 1 following **that rescission or the date on which that matter arises, as the case may be**."* **Two departures from the §4 draft, both deliberate and neither a change of substance.** The draft said *"following **it**"*; a pronoun with two candidate antecedents in a deadline is the kind of thing this file has had rot before, so the antecedent is named. And the draft's *"to state in that report"* is given its full referent, *"in the report required by this subdivision"*, so the duty does not depend on which report *"that"* picks up. **[MOO-152](/MOO/issues/MOO-152) owns store revision 52 and is closed; this is the first time I have touched J 1**, and it is on the Director's direction, not on my own motion.

114. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 57, from [MOO-152](/MOO/issues/MOO-152)'s pass 17, finding S-1 — and it is the most serious thing on this list.** The G 4 settlement bar has two limbs, and store revision 52 disapplied both **only inside the two sentences it wrote them in**. Two routes still reach the release limb and the first is ordinary, not exotic. **(a)** A member whose purchase is reduced on a **D 6 recertification** has had her reservation released *"to the extent of any reduction"* **in accordance with D 7**; she pays in full, is credited, and dies before retiring. Clause (i) applies and the Board credits the credit and pays the death benefit — but **no rescission is involved, so the item-89 cure never reaches her**, and on the bar's face the subsidy is never settled: subsection N never pays the trust, the remaining reservation is charged against the $3,000,000 limit **permanently** under D 3, and her election is in none of J 1 limb (a)'s classes, so the reporting duty is perpetual. **That is the fatal item 89 cured, reached through a different door.** **(b)** The involuntary-separation rescission has no *notwithstanding* of its own, and its deeming defeats the determination limb while saying nothing about the release. **The ground is this record's own:** §6.0-52 item 1 and both cures are premised on the strict historical-fact reading of the bar, and a drafter who legislates twice on that reading cannot rely on a purposive one elsewhere. **Should the bar be amended once instead?** — *released **in full** … **and has not been reinstated under this subdivision***, and *taken effect **and has not been rescinded, reversed, or modified***. That cures both routes and makes the two existing *notwithstanding* clauses surplusage rather than load-bearing. **Recommend enacting.** Candidate text verbatim in [`moo152-pass17-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-moo152-pass17-disposition) §2. **It settles subsidies the bar on its face refuses, so it spends money — which is why it is here and not in the text. Enacted nowhere.** **ANSWERED AND CLOSED IN STORE REVISION 60: the Director answered ENACT on the question card of 12 October 2026, and the single amendment to the bar is in the text — *"released in full in accordance with subdivision D 7 and has not been reinstated under this subdivision"*, and *"has taken effect and has not been rescinded, reversed, or modified."* Both surviving routes are closed, and the two existing *notwithstanding* clauses are left standing as surplusage rather than deleted (§6.0-60 item 5). **It settles subsidies the bar on its face refused, which is the money the Director took on this card.** This item is not an open question.**

115. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 57, from pass 17's S-2 — and it is the Director's own question answered against the text.** Item 93's reversal duty is **unconditioned**: it does not ask whether clause (i), (ii) or (iii) applies. A member who seeks reconsideration and **loses** — the Board modifies the determination to a **larger** reduction — satisfies it on its face, so the Board must *"recertify and settle the certified subsidy"* within 60 days and subsection N pays it. **Subdivision 2 is the non-settlement outcome; the sentence now pays the subsidy anyway.** The charitable reading is that *"as this subdivision requires"* is a manner qualifier and the duty is empty where nothing is due — **but then the sentence's own double *notwithstanding* has nothing to operate on. Either it is a null duty and the repair was unnecessary, or it is an unpriced money command; one clause cures both.** **Should the settlement limb be conditioned** — *"where the determination as reversed or modified is such that the certified subsidy falls to be settled under subdivision 1 or under this subdivision"* — and the referent corrected to **subdivision 1** for the member who did retire in a qualifying retirement? **Recommend enacting.** Candidate text in the companion record §2. **Enacted nowhere.** **ANSWERED AND CLOSED IN STORE REVISION 60: ENACT. The settlement limb now reads *"where the determination as reversed or modified is such that the certified subsidy falls to be settled under subdivision 1 or under this subdivision"*, and the referent is corrected. This item is not an open question.**

116. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 57, from pass 17's S-3, and it is the one that moves the most money.** The arrears formula the Director confirmed as item 105 **is right and does not underpay her** — the review worked the modification-in-her-favour case and the uplift is correct. **The defect runs the other way: the formula is gross, not net.** Its words were lifted from subdivision 2's arrears sentence, safe there **because subdivision 2 forbids any payment before its determination takes effect** — nothing had been paid, so gross equalled net. Item 93 transplanted it into a context where payments have been **running throughout**. On the review's facts: shortfall **$8,280**, amount commanded **$64,080**, excess **$55,800** — and **G 4's own no-collection sentence** bars recovery (**not** subsection F's, whose limb is tied to the failure to retire; that citation in §6.0-52 item 4 and in item 105 is corrected, and the conclusion is stronger for it). **Should the formula pay the shortfall rather than the gross** — *"every amount **by which the amounts paid to the member fall short of the amounts that** would have been payable"* — **with a second limb for a determination reversed in its entirety**, which otherwise has no *"took effect"* referent and pays zero? **Recommend enacting both limbs.** Candidate text in the companion record §2. **Enacted nowhere.** **ANSWERED AND CLOSED IN STORE REVISION 60: BOTH LIMBS. The formula pays *"every amount by which the amounts paid to the member fall short of the amounts that would have been payable"*, and a determination reversed in its entirety is computed as though no determination had been made. This item is not an open question.**

117. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 57, from pass 17's S-4. The carve-out rescues the *late* filer and excludes the *timely* one.** Four classes of disability approval after a determination has taken effect: **(1)** filed on or before the effective date — rescission, pre-existing; **(2)** filed after, § 51.1-156 H's **mandatory** waiver required — rescission, the Director's item-91 answer; **(3)** filed after, waived under H's **permissive** first sentence — nothing, by the Director's choice; **(4)** filed after the determination took effect **but inside § 51.1-156 A or B's own ninety days, so no waiver is needed at all** — **nothing, and nobody decided that.** Class 4 complied with the Code's own deadline and is treated worse than a member who missed it by eight months. **That inversion is the one live constitutional surface** — equal protection, pleaded alongside an Article 5 petition; the Commonwealth would probably win on rational basis, but **there is no reason in the record** why class 4 fares worse than class 2. **Second defect in the same words:** the trigger is *"as to which the Board **was required** … to waive"* — a counterfactual about the Board's legal obligation, which the section nowhere assigns, times, or requires notice of, so a member will argue the Board *was* required whatever it called its waiver. **Should both clauses go in** — close class 4 by reading on *"an application filed within a period set forth in subsection A or B of § 51.1-156"*, and anchor the trigger to the **Medical Board's certification** rather than to a legal conclusion? **Recommend both**, and **class 3 should be re-made knowing class 4 exists.** Candidate text in the companion record §2. **Enacted nowhere.** **ANSWERED AND CLOSED IN STORE REVISION 60: BOTH CLAUSES, with clause (a) drafted WIDER than carded.** Enacted: *"an application the filing of which did not require a waiver of a ninety-day notification period set forth in subsection A or B of § 51.1-156"*, because the carded words reached only the member inside the ninety days and missed the member whose final qualifying retirement date passed **while she was still in service**, for whom § 51.1-156 A sets no period at all (§6.0-60 item 1). The trigger is anchored to *"a certification described in clause (ii) of subsection H of § 51.1-156"* (§6.0-60 item 2). **Class 3, the discretionary waiver, remains excluded**, which is the line the Director drew on item 91 and re-made knowing class 4 existed. This item is not an open question.**

118. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 57, from pass 17's S-5 — the gaming path, and it is also why J 1's terminal date can still never arrive.** The 120-day aggregate caps **subsection M requests** and nothing else; **disability-application pendency is uncapped and uncounted.** While any application is on file the Board is **forbidden** to make the subdivision 2 determination and **no period runs** — so a member who files, is denied, and files again indefinitely is never reduced. D 7's second sentence withholds release while vesting is undetermined, so her reservation stays charged against the **$3,000,000** limit, **until a fresh Director of Planning and Budget confirmation is obtained the Board may accept no further election** — the waiting list bears that — and J 1 limb (a) is false forever, so the annual report never ends. **Two parts: (a) extend the 120-day aggregate to disability applications filed after the first; (b) give limb (a) a long-stop** — *"in no event … after the first December 1 following the twentieth anniversary of the expiration of this section."* **Recommend both.** Candidate text in the companion record §2. **Enacted nowhere.** **ANSWERED AND CLOSED IN STORE REVISION 60: BOTH.** The 120-day aggregate now counts *"applications for a disability retirement allowance filed after the first such application"*, and J 1 closes with *"In no event shall the Board be required to report under this subdivision after the first December 1 following the twentieth anniversary of the expiration of this section"* — placed as the **last sentence of J 1** rather than inside limb (a), so that it reaches the further report as well (§6.0-60 item 7). **Store revision 59's enactment of item 113 gave that further report an independent trigger, which makes the long-stop more necessary and not less.** This item is not an open question.**

119. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 57, from pass 17's S-6 — and it is against the Director's item-94 answer, which is why it is a question and not a repair.** The confinement's **substance** is declaratory of § 51.1-155.1's own conditions and admits nobody; that holds and the review verified it. **But it carries a *temporal* condition § 51.1-155.1 does not contain**, and item 94 pushed that condition out of one definition and into **the whole section**. A member certified at 48 and involuntarily separated at 50 is told by G 4's hardship branch to retire in a **qualifying retirement** within 90 days; her only unreduced route is § 51.1-155.1 A; and the residual sentence resolves the operative date to the decision *"in which that date is applied"* — her 2030 certification, when she had not been separated. **So she cannot take the retirement the hardship branch requires of her, she fails subsection F, and subdivision 2 reduces her credit. Before item 94 this could not happen.** The same residual sentence yields **no date at all** in a G 1 settlement, a qualifying-retirement determination or the hardship branch (pass 17's M-4), so one clause is both cures: *"or, where this sentence is applied otherwise than in the determination of the member's unreduced retirement eligibility date, on or before the member's effective date of retirement."* **Recommend enacting.** The alternative — revert to *"this definition"* and amend the twin expressly — is the option §6.0-52 declined, and **the ground given for declining it does not survive this scenario.** Candidate text in the companion record §2. **Enacted nowhere.** **ANSWERED AND CLOSED IN STORE REVISION 60: ENACT. The residual temporal rule is in the definition — *"or, where this sentence is applied otherwise than in the determination of the member's unreduced retirement eligibility date, on or before the member's effective date of retirement"* — and it is also pass 17's M-4 cure. The new limb is disjunctive, so it does not displace the definition's third sentence (§5.39 item 11). This item is not an open question.**

120. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 57. Two pre-existing gaps that the Director's own answers made load-bearing, which is why neither is a repair.** **(a)** From pass 17's M-5: subsection E's **crediting** bar is excepted only *"as subdivision G 4 provides in the case of the member's **death**"*, yet G 4's vesting sentence credits the whole purchased credit on **clause (ii)** and **clause (iii)** too, where payment may be incomplete — and items 91 and 92 bring a new population into clause (iii). **(b)** Found on re-verification, not by the reviewer: the **death branch's own** adverse-tax fallback applies an unpayable refund *"to the purchase of additional service credit **for the member**"* where the member is dead — the same defect R2 cured in the rescission branch, in text no answer reached. **Should E's exception name clauses (ii) and (iii), and should the death branch get R2's words?** **Recommend both.** Candidate text in the companion record §2. **Enacted nowhere.** **ANSWERED AND CLOSED IN STORE REVISION 60: BOTH SELECTED AND BOTH ENACTED — but (a) DOES NOT DO WHAT THIS ITEM SAID IT WOULD, and the reader should know that before relying on it.** Pass 18's S18-6 shows the premise was false: outside death and the rescission branch **G 4 imposes no crediting duty at all**, so the widened exception lifts a bar in front of a duty that does not exist. It is enacted on the narrower ground that subsection E's death-only exception conflicted on its face with G 4's unqualified *"Subsection E does not prevent the crediting of service credit required by this subdivision"* (§6.0-60 item 9). **The missing crediting duty is item 126 and is enacted nowhere.** (b) is enacted as a **conjunctive duty rather than as an exception**, because in the death branch the member has always died, which also cures pass 18's M18-3 (§6.0-60 item 4).**

121. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 57, from pass 17's M-7, and it is the money-side twin of item 100.** Four separate 60-day payment duties — item 97's refund, D 6's, subdivision 2's, G 5's and the death branch's — are each a bare *"shall pay … within 60 days."* **Subsection M 2 reaches a determination of the Board and a failure to make a determination or to give a notice within a period required by this section; a failure to *pay* is neither**, and no J 1 line records it. So a late refund has **no remedy inside the section at all**. **Should "or to make a payment" go into M 2's failure clause, and should interest run at the rate subdivision B 4 a already defines?** **Recommend the M 2 limb** — it costs nothing and gives four duties an *"or else"*. **The interest limb is a money decision and I make no recommendation on it.** **Enacted nowhere.** **ANSWERED AND CLOSED IN STORE REVISION 60: the M 2 LIMB ONLY.** Subsection M 2's failure clause now reads *"to make a determination, to give a notice, or to make a payment"*, and its reconsideration-timing limb is conformed to it. **THE INTEREST LIMB WAS NOT SELECTED AND IS NOT IN THE TEXT.** The conforming extension of subdivision J 1's failure line was outside the option selected and is carded as item 127.**

122. **ANSWERED IN PART by the Policy Director on 12 October 2026 and ENACTED IN STORE REVISION 61: (2)(i), (3), (4), (6) and (7) — the cap restoration measured by what has been paid under subsection N, the reinstatement on review, the release trigger where no allowance is payable, the state agency's 30-day death notice, and the redetermination clock run from the date the Board learns of the death. STILL OPEN AND NOT RE-ASKED: (1) F5's two forms, (2)(ii), (5) F12's period, (8) F15's payee and (9) the § 51.1-165 C 2 displacement.** Originally from [MOO-154](/MOO/issues/MOO-154): nine answers drafted in full, four of which decide who bears a loss. Each is drafted word for word at [`moo154-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-97#document-moo154-disposition) §7 so it can be enacted with one substitution. Items 60 (the elected-option class), 106 and 107 are unaffected and stay open.
    - **(1) F5 — the unreduced-retirement-eligibility bar. This is the ERF residual [MOO-97](/MOO/issues/MOO-97) was opened on, and it is the largest thing on this card.** Subsection E's last sentence has two limbs; G 4's saving reaches only the **crediting** limb, so the second limb — *"no member shall be treated as having reached his unreduced retirement eligibility date by reason of an election before payment in full is received"* — stands unqualified in the death case. Clause (v) of *"eligible member"* means **every** member in G 4 is one whose unreduced eligibility depends on the purchase. So the survivor's § 51.1-162 B allowance may be computed **with** the § 51.1-155 A 2 or A 3 reduction while the certified subsidy was priced on the member's *"anticipated earliest qualifying retirement date"* — i.e. on unreduced retirement. **The Commonwealth pays for the unreduced outcome and the survivor is paid the reduced one.** Drafted two ways: **Form A** extends G 4's saving to every member whom the credited service carries over the line; **Form B** extends it only where the member had completed payment in full before death. **A costs more than B and B leaves the partial payer reduced. Neither is enacted and the figure is Fiscal's.**
    - **(2) F9(a) — the $3,000,000 cap stops measuring money.** Subsection N pays the settled amount to the retirement system **on settlement**, which G 4 fixes at the date of death; answer 7 of your 12 October card then restores that amount to the cap, and nothing moves it back. The Program's cash exposure becomes the cap **plus every released-and-already-paid amount, with no stated bound**. Three answers: **(i)** restore to the cap only to the extent the amount has **not** been paid under subsection N; **(ii)** require repayment to the appropriation (this takes money back out of the trust fund after settlement — [MOO-96](/MOO/issues/MOO-96)'s F-3 and Va. Const. art. X, § 11 both bear on it); **(iii)** leave it, and state it in the fiscal note and the memo. **(iii) is a decision and not a default.**
    - **(3) F9(b) — a survivor who wins her review has nothing to be paid out of.** G 4 forbids a second settlement and its reinstatement machinery is keyed to a released **reservation**, not to a released **settled amount**. Drafted: one sentence reinstating the released amount, with the contingency released with it, where the determination is reversed or modified on reconsideration or on judicial review, and disapplying the once-only rule to it. **It restores a right and it costs what the right costs.**
    - **(4) F10 and F11's release half — the asymmetry between the election case and the no-election case.** The release fires only on an **election** under § 51.1-162; under § 51.1-162 **A** there is no election, so the unmarried member with no minor child and no surviving parent — identical economics, no allowance ever payable — frees nothing. Drafted: trigger the release where **no retirement allowance is payable** under § 51.1-162 on the death, which also cures the two-minor-children case where one guardian elects and an allowance is still payable to the other child. **It widens what the cap gives back, so it interacts with (2) and should be decided with it.**
    - **(5) F12's alternative — state a period in G 4 for the § 51.1-162 B election.** § 51.1-162 B states none; this revision makes the Board's statement conditional rather than inventing one. Drafted: 90 days from the notice, with 30 days for the Board to act. **It closes (2)'s open-ended driver and it takes a right from a survivor who is slow.**
    - **(6) F13(c) — nothing requires anyone to tell the Board the member died.** The crediting deadline runs from when the Board *"learns of"* the death. Drafted: the 30-day state-agency notice duty with a subsection J line, on the model of G 4's own involuntary-separation limb. **It is a new duty on employing agencies and a Dillon's Rule grant question for any employer that is not a state agency.**
    - **(7) F13(e) — a deadline that can expire before the Board can begin.** The redetermination runs 90 days from the **date of death**; where the Board learns of the death later than that, it is already late. Drafted: run it from the date the Board learns of the death in the death case only. **It is a timing change to a period you set.**
    - **(8) F15's payment side — a refund payable to an estate that may never exist.** G 4 directs the refund and the notice to *"the member's estate"*, and nothing in the act, in Chapter 1 or in Title 64.2 requires a personal representative to qualify. Drafted: pay and notify the person who received the member's accumulated contributions under § 51.1-162, or in that section's order of precedence, where none has qualified. **It names a payee, which is why it is yours and not mine.**
    - **(9) F1's alternative — displace subdivision C 2 of § 51.1-165 instead of preserving it.** What is enacted pays the **greater** of C 2's benefit and (f)'s. The alternative names C 2 in the notwithstanding and deletes the greater-of sentence: simpler, one sentence shorter, and it takes an existing right from a spouse, minor child or parent named under a joint and last-survivor option. **One substitution either way.**

123. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 59, out of answering §7 items 88 and 110 together. Drafting hygiene, no policy content, and I have enacted nothing.** **§ 2.2-4021 is in Article 3 of the Administrative Process Act** — verified at source on 12 October 2026, Article 3 (Case Decisions), §§ 2.2-4020 through 2.2-4022 as published. Item 88's optional limb therefore disapplies § 2.2-4021 **in its entirety** to a reconsideration under subdivision M 2, and the new subdivision M 3 **separately** disapplies § 2.2-4021 B's and C's deemed-decision sentences *"to a determination of the Board under this section **or to a reconsideration under subdivision 2**."* **M 3's reconsideration limb is now redundant.** **I have kept it, and the reasons are three.** It was directed in those words. It is independently necessary for the other half of its reach — *"a determination of the Board under this section"* — which the limb does not touch and which is where counsel found the exposure **already live**. And it survives the removal of the limb, which is the thing most likely to be revisited. **The risk of keeping it is a negative-implication argument**: a reader could ask why M 3 names reconsiderations at all if Article 3 is already gone, and answer that the limb must not reach § 2.2-4021 after all. I think the argument fails — belt-and-braces disapplication is common and M 3's words are flatly inconsistent with it — but it is an argument the draft creates for free. **Options:** **(a)** leave both as enacted, **which I recommend**; **(b)** drop *"or to a reconsideration under subdivision 2"* from M 3, leaving M 3 to determinations and the limb to reconsiderations, which is tidier and breaks if the limb is ever dropped; **(c)** drop the limb and rely on M 3, which reverses the Director's answer to item 88 and is not mine to propose on hygiene grounds. **One substitution either way.**

124. **ANSWERED BY THE BOARD 12 OCTOBER 2026 — OPTION (d), THE MEMBER-TRIGGERED DENIAL — AND ENACTED IN STORE REVISION 63. Two sentences in subdivision M 2; the verbatim words, the three repairs built into them and the one residual that is not cured are at [`bill-text-s8`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-bill-text-s8) §0.0-63, §5.42 and §6.0-63. [MOO-141](/MOO/issues/MOO-141)'s gate 2 is cured. CLOSED; the record below is retained.** **NEW IN STORE REVISION 59, out of the Director's answer to §7 item 88 read against the answer to item 110. This is the one gap the two answers open, it is a provision nobody has agreed, and it is not enacted.** Option (d) makes the **completed** reconsideration the case decision. The optional limb disapplies Article 3, which contains § 2.2-4021. Subdivision M 3 disapplies § 2.2-4021 B and C to a reconsideration in terms. **So after store revision 59 a reconsideration the Board simply never completes produces no case decision at all, and § 2.2-4026 A gives the court action only to a party aggrieved by one.** Before this revision the position was *uncertain* — counsel called it *"a residual § 2.2-4021 question about a reconsideration the Board sits on"* — and it is now **settled against the member** by two express exclusions. **What she still has:** M 2's 90-day duty to act; the reportability of the failure under subsection C's reporting sentence and under subdivision J 1; and, where the thing she sought reconsideration of was itself a failure within a period, a second request is barred from tolling but the underlying failure remains reviewable. **What she does not have is a route into court.** **Drafted cure, as a new fourth sentence of subdivision M 2, after the 90-day sentence:** > <u>Where the Board does not act upon a request for reconsideration within the 90 days allowed by this subsection, the request is denied on the last day of that period, and that denial is a final determination of the Board upon the reconsideration for the purposes of this subsection.</u> **Why this form.** It creates a **reviewable** decision and not a **favourable** one, so it does none of the work subdivision M 3 was enacted to prevent: nothing is deemed in the member's favour, no subsidy is charged against the $170,000 or the $3,000,000 by the Board's silence, and the Director of Planning and Budget's confirmation is not bypassed. It uses the section's own vocabulary — *"final determination … upon the reconsideration"* is option (d)'s own predicate — so it needs no further words to become a case decision. And it names the date, so the 30-day Article 5 clock has something to run from. **Options:** **(a)** nothing, and the gap stands on the record; **(b)** the sentence above, **which I recommend**; **(c)** the same sentence with a longer period, if 90 days plus the Board's existing workload is thought too tight. **One sentence either way. No figure moves on any of them.**

    **ADDENDUM, 12 October 2026 — the premise re-verified against the live body at store revision 61, three repairs the carded sentence needs, and a new option (d). STILL ENACTED NOWHERE.** Occasioned by the Policy Director's comment of 17:28 UTC making this item a first-class blocker edge on [MOO-141](/MOO/issues/MOO-141) (its gate 2). Read directly from the document store — document revision 76, store revision 61, 271,611 characters, enacted §1 at offsets 56,843 to 168,807 — and not from this document's own reproductions.

    - **The premise holds and both exclusions are live.** In the enacted text: *"The Board shall act upon a request for reconsideration within 90 days."* — **1** occurrence. *"Articles 3 (§ 2.2-4018 et seq.) and 4 (§ 2.2-4024) of the Administrative Process Act shall not apply to a reconsideration under this subsection."* — **1** occurrence in §1 (the second occurrence in this document is §0 commentary, at offset 38,589). Subdivision M 3's disapplication of the deemed-in-favour sentences of § 2.2-4021 B and C reaches *"a determination of the Board under this section or to a reconsideration under subdivision 2"* — **1** occurrence. **So a reconsideration the Board never completes still produces no case decision, and § 2.2-4026 A still gives the court action only to a party aggrieved by one.** Store revision 61 substituted thirteen times in G 4, D 1 and M 2 and moved none of this.

    - **REPAIR 1 — the carded sentence does not reach the case-decision sentence it relies on, and that is a defect in my own card.** This item says the cure *"uses the section's own vocabulary … so it needs no further words to become a case decision."* **It does not.** The live sentence is keyed twice to events a deemed denial does not produce: *"**Upon completion** of a reconsideration under this subsection, the final determination **issued by** the Board shall constitute a case decision as defined in § 2.2-4001."* Nothing is completed and nothing is issued. On that reading the cure gives the member a denial with a date and still no route into court, which is the whole of what it exists to supply. **The repair is express characterisation, and it is now safe in a way it was not at item 88:** my ground for refusing item 88's option (a) was that characterising Board inaction as a case decision opens § 2.2-4021's statutory default against the Board's own limitations — and **M 2's last sentence and M 3 now close that default by express text**, so the characterisation costs nothing it used to cost. Add to the new sentence: *"and that denial constitutes the final determination of the Board upon the reconsideration and a case decision as defined in § 2.2-4001."*

    - **REPAIR 2 — the Director's "91st day" is better than the carded "last day of that period", and I take the correction.** On the carded words the denial falls on **day 90**, which is a day on which the Board may still lawfully act, so the section would carry a denial and a live power to act on the same date and two inconsistent determinations could exist on one day. Denying on the **first day after the expiration of the 90 days** puts the denial on the first day on which the Board is in breach. **Cost: one day.**

    - **REPAIR 3 — the denial is silent, and this section's own convention is that the member's clocks start when she is told.** M 2 keys her 30-day reconsideration window to *"after the notice of the determination is given"*, and a **completed** reconsideration is *"written reconsideration by the Board stating the reasons for its decision."* A deemed denial has no writing, no reasons and no notice. Whether that silence also starts an Article 5 period she cannot see turns on **Rule 2A:2, which § 2.2-4026 does not state** — subsection A commits the period to the Rules of the Supreme Court of Virginia and subsection C fixes an appeal date only for regulations (verified at source, 12 October 2026). **I have not retrieved Rule 2A:2 and I assert nothing about its content.** [MOO-186](/MOO/issues/MOO-186) asks Statutory Researcher. **It does not gate this answer, because both branches are adverse:** if the period runs from the decision, her court window opens and closes without her knowledge; if it runs from service of a final order, there is no order to serve and she may have no reviewable decision at all.

    - **NEW OPTION (d) — mine, drafted and enacted nowhere, and it is what I now recommend over (b).** Make the denial **member-triggered**, on the Code's own device. § 2.2-4021 B reaches a deemed decision only **after the party files written notice** that the period has expired; (d) borrows that structure and reverses the outcome, so the member creates the date and therefore knows it, and nothing is deemed in her favour:

      > <u>Where the Board does not act upon a request for reconsideration within the 90 days allowed by this subsection, the person who filed the request may file with the Board written notice that the period has expired. Where the Board does not act upon the request within 30 days after that notice is filed, the request is denied on the first day after the expiration of those 30 days, and that denial constitutes the final determination of the Board upon the reconsideration and a case decision as defined in § 2.2-4001 for the purposes of this subsection.</u>

      **What (d) costs:** up to 30 further days before she may go to court, and a member who never files the notice never gets a decision — the same bargain § 2.2-4021 B strikes against every party before every agency in the Commonwealth. **What it buys:** she always knows the date. (b) and (c) cannot give her that without a notice duty, and the Board that is already silent would breach the notice duty too.

    - **One coupling to record.** (b), (c) and (d) all use *"the 90 days allowed by this subsection"*, which is the phrase **[MOO-141](/MOO/issues/MOO-141) card (j)** is about — **1** occurrence in the live body, in M 2's tolling clause (ii). Whichever way card (j) is decided the new sentence must move with it, or be worded to avoid the phrase.

    - **"Pass 18's card C" does not resolve to anything, and option (a) therefore has no drafted fallback.** [MOO-141](/MOO/issues/MOO-141)'s gate 2 names *"§7 item 124's deemed denial on the 91st day, or pass 18's card C if item 124 is declined."* Pass 18 is [MOO-156](/MOO/issues/MOO-156) and carries **no lettered cards** — 6 serious (S18-1 to S18-6), 11 moderate (M18-1 to M18-11), 3 minor, 2 adjacent, and §7 hand-offs; it does not mention § 2.2-4021, Article 5 or the no-decision residual anywhere. MOO-141's own card list does have a **card (c)**, but it is G 2's two undefined uses of *"pending"*, which is a different defect. **So on the present record, declining this item leaves gate 2 with no cure to apply** — it would then be cleared only by the Director accepting the residual on the record, not by any drafting of mine.

    - **No figure moves on any option.** None of (a) to (d) admits a member, changes a quantity, or charges anything against the $3,000,000 or the $170,000.

125. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 59. The §7 item list is now a separate document, and that changes what the Editor must be given.** Item 112 is answered and the split is done: [`bill-text-s8-item-list`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-bill-text-s8-item-list) holds items 1 to 125 and the bill document holds §0 to §6 and §8. **Counsel built the opposition's floor speech out of this list** and that was the Director's reason for splitting it. **The split is not a containment control and I will not describe it as one.** Both documents are Paperclip issue documents on [MOO-42](/MOO/issues/MOO-42), both are pre-decisional and internal, and anyone who can read one can read the other. What the split buys is that **a release of the bill text no longer carries the item list by accident** — it is now a separate act to attach it. **What the Director still has to decide, and I make no recommendation because it is a release question and not a drafting one:** whether the item list goes to the [Editor](/MOO/agents/editor) at all, whether it goes to counsel in full (every counsel pass so far has read it and three of them have used it), and what happens to it when the bill goes to a patron or to the Division of Legislative Services, where a 308,000-character internal list of unresolved questions attached to a 112,886-character bill would be read as the drafter's own case against it. **Enacted nowhere; this item is a question.**

126. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 60, from [MOO-156](/MOO/issues/MOO-156)'s pass 18, finding S18-6 — and it is the live hole item 120(a) was carded to close and does not.** Item 120(a) is enacted, and subsection E's crediting-bar exception now names a disability approval and an error of the Board as well as death. **But its stated ground was wrong and the review is right:** G 4 imposes **no crediting duty at all** outside death and the rescission branch. Clause (ii) and clause (iii) provide only that subdivisions 2 and 3 *"shall not apply"* and that the purchased credit *"shall vest"* — and **vesting is not crediting**; worse, switching off subdivision 3 switches off the only partial-crediting duty the section has. **Worked case:** a member certified for 24 months pays for 9 and is approved for a disability retirement allowance before the completion date. Clause (ii) applies. The certified subsidy is settled on the effective date of the allowance and **the Commonwealth pays it over to the retirement system under subsection N** — and the member is credited with **nothing**, no provision requiring it, with item 120(a) having lifted a bar in front of a duty that does not exist. **Should G 4 carry a crediting duty for the clause (ii) and clause (iii) partial payer, in the rescission branch's own words?** Candidate: *"Where clause (ii) or (iii) of this subdivision applies and the member has not completed payment, the Board shall credit the member with the largest number of full or partial months of service credit for which full payment has been made, determined in the manner provided in subsection F of § 51.1-142.2 and without regard to the method by which payment was made, valued at the cost determined under subdivision B 4 of § 51.1-142.2; shall recertify the certified subsidy to reflect the service credit so credited, and subdivision D 6 applies to that recertification only so far as it provides for the reservation of an increase; and shall refund, to the member or, where the member has died, to the member's estate, and to any employer that paid on the member's behalf in proportion to the amounts each paid, any amount paid that exceeds the cost of the service credit so credited."* **Recommend enacting.** It decides what a partial payer is credited with and what she is refunded, which is a money question and not mine. **The conflict between subsection E's exception and G 4's unqualified *"Subsection E does not prevent the crediting of service credit required by this subdivision"* should be resolved in the same pass.** **Enacted nowhere.**

127. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 60. Mine, and it is a one-clause conform the Director's bundle answer did not reach.** Item 121's M 2 limb is enacted: a failure *"to make a payment"* within a period required by the section is now reviewable, which gives five bare 60-day payment duties an *"or else"*. **Subdivision J 1's failure line was not extended with it** — it still reports *"every failure of the Board to make a determination, or to give a notice, within a period required by this section"* — so the remedy is now wider than the report, and the money committees cannot see the failures the member may now complain of. The bundle option the Director selected named M 2 alone, so **I have not touched J 1.** **Should J 1's failure line read *"to make a determination, to give a notice, or to make a payment"*?** Nine words. **Recommend enacting**; item 99's quantification will be read against these lines. **Enacted nowhere.**

128. **ANSWERED BY THE POLICY DIRECTOR ON 12 OCTOBER 2026 AND ENACTED IN FULL IN STORE REVISION 62 — ALL SEVEN. CLOSED; THIS ITEM IS NOT AN OPEN QUESTION.** The Director selected **f3-a, f3-b, f7-toll, f10-b, f11-disclose, f14-supp and m2-lateness** — every option on the card except f3-c and f8-gated, which were alternatives to options selected. What each became, with the two places the enacted words differ from the card: **(1) f3-a and f3-b are cumulative and are enacted as one clause** in subdivision G 4's greater-of sentence. **A residual the card did not name is now §7 item 129 answer 2: § 51.1-163's own measure has no content in a pre-effective-date death, so the approved comparison has one incomputable side.** **(2) f7-toll** is enacted in subdivision G 4, **with its anchor reworded** to *"a notice required by this subdivision on the death of a member"*, because *"the notice required by this subdivision"* is ambiguous — the subdivision requires five notices. It is **the act's first operative override of a period stated in a section this act does not amend**, and the conforming amendment is §7 item 129 answer 7. **(3) f14-supp** and **(4) m2-lateness** are enacted as drafted. **(5) f11-disclose** is enacted in subsection N. **(6) f10-b is enacted in subsection L in the chapter form, not the three-named-system form the card carried**, because the definition of *"eligible member"* already excludes *"a member of a retirement system established under Chapter 2 (§ 51.1-200 et seq.), Chapter 2.1 (§ 51.1-211 et seq.), or Chapter 3 (§ 51.1-300 et seq.)"* and two formulations of one concept is a defect — **so the scope decision was already the law of the act and the enacted sentence confirms it rather than changing it**; §6.0-62 item 12. The reasoning below is retained as the record of the choice, **not** as a live question.

    **NEW IN STORE REVISION 61, from [MOO-161](/MOO/issues/MOO-161) — seven answers drafted in full and enacted nowhere.** Each is drafted word for word at [`moo161-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-97#document-moo161-disposition) §7 so it can be enacted with one substitution. **Three of them move money between named people and I have not picked one.** Items 60, 106, 107 and the five unenacted answers at item 122 are unaffected and stay open.
    - **(1) F3 — what the greater-of is measured on, and who is paid it. The sharpest thing in the pass after F1.** Subdivision G 4 directs the Board to pay *"the benefit it provides or the benefit determined under the preceding sentence, whichever is greater."* The two sides are **different kinds of money payable to different people**: C 2's first limb is a lump sum payable, through § 51.1-162 A's manner, to the designated beneficiary or a surviving relative by order of precedence; the act's side is a monthly allowance payable to § 51.1-162 B's first qualifying person. On the reviewer's facts a member names her **mother** as contingent annuitant, is survived by a **husband**, and has her **sister** as designated beneficiary: three payees, one direction to pay *"whichever is greater,"* and no basis of comparison. Three answers, any combination: **(a)** state the measure — *"the two being compared on the basis of actuarial equivalence as of the date of the member's death determined on the assumptions then in use by the Board"*; **(b)** state the payee — *"and the Board shall pay it to the person to whom the provision under which the greater benefit is determined requires it to be paid"*; **(c)** confine the greater-of to the case where the payees coincide, which is C 2's own *"however"* limb, and let C 2's first limb be displaced. **(c) is the smallest and it takes a right from the designated beneficiary; (b) names a payee, which is why it is yours.**
    - **(2) F7 — the compelled notice of a right that may already have expired. The reviewer's highest-value repair after F1.** § 51.1-162 C's waiver must be made *"within ninety days after the death of the member."* G 4's notice is due at the outer limit 90 days after the Board **learns** of the death (60 to credit, 30 to notify). **Every day the Board takes to learn of the death is a day by which the Commonwealth's own notice of the right can post-date the expiry of it**, and the person who loses by it has no remedy, because the Board gave the notice inside the period the act allows. Drafted: *"Where the Board gives the notice required by this subdivision later than 30 days after the death of the member, the period stated in subsection C of § 51.1-162 within which a waiver must be made does not expire before the thirtieth day after the notice is given to the person entitled to make it."* **It overrides a period in another section, which is why it is yours and not mine.** The state agency's new 30-day death notice, enacted in this revision, shortens the exposure but does not close it.
    - **(3) F10 — whether a State Police, VaLORS or Judicial Retirement System member is in this Program at all.** § 51.1-142.2's purchase right runs to *"any member in service"*; subsection L excludes teachers and employees of political subdivisions and **does not exclude the sister systems**; § 51.1-200 applies Chapter 1 to the State Police Officers' Retirement System *"except as otherwise provided."* The act's death branch names **§ 51.1-162 alone**, while § 51.1-163 names §§ 51.1-162, 51.1-207 and 51.1-218 *"in accordance with the retirement plan covering such member."* Two answers: **(a)** generalise, in subdivision G 4 and in D 1's certification, to *"§ 51.1-162, § 51.1-207, or § 51.1-218, as applicable to the retirement plan covering the member"*; or **(b)** confine the Program to Chapter 1 members by one sentence in subsection L, which is cheaper and is a scope decision. **Routed to the Statutory Researcher for the list of sister-system death-benefit sections before either is enacted.**
    - **(4) F11 — say expressly that the death benefit is outside the cap, or leave it to inference.** Every cost control in the act is addressed to the *certified subsidy*: D 2's $3,000,000 and $170,000 limits, subsection K's kill-switch, subsection N's appropriation. **A benefit determined under G 4 is none of those things; it falls on the trust fund.** Drafted for subsection N: *"Nothing in this section charges a benefit payable under § 51.1-162 against the limitations in subdivision D 2."* **It changes nothing and discloses everything**, which is the argument for and against it. F1's cure removes the large exposure; what remains is the C 2 greater-of, which the reviewer priced at about one case every three years.
    - **(5) F14's companion — a supplemental notice where the Board later determines an amount differently.** Entitlement can be clear while the amount is not: a work-related death's § 51.1-162 C allowance waits on a Workers' Compensation award. This revision defers the notice until the amount is determined; the drafted sentence adds a **further** notice, with a fresh review period, where the Board afterwards determines the amount differently. **It is a new duty on the Board and was not agreed.**
    - **(6) M 2 — make lateness reviewable after it is cured.** The failure limb closes *"at any time before the Board makes the determination or gives the notice,"* so a member whose crediting was 30 days late has no remedy for the lateness the moment it is cured. Drafted: *"or, where the Board has made the determination or given the notice late, within 30 days after it does so."* **Small, and it is a new right.**
    - **(7) F8's alternative — keep the general notice class, gated.** This revision replaced *"other person to whom this section requires a notice to be given"* with two named classes, because the formula reached the public through subsection K. If the Director wants the general formula back, the gated form is *"or other person to whom this section requires a notice of a determination affecting that person to be given."* **Not enacted; the narrow form is in the text.**

129. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 62, from [MOO-169](/MOO/issues/MOO-169) — seven answers drafted in full and enacted nowhere.** Each is one substitution against the live text of store revision 62 and each is drafted word for word at [`moo169-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-97#document-moo169-disposition) §7. **Four of the seven decide who bears a loss and I have picked none.** Items 60, 106, 107, 126 and 127, and the five unenacted answers at item 122, are unaffected and stay open.

    **ANSWER (4) IS ANSWERED AND ENACTED — store revision 65, 12 October 2026, and it is the only one of the seven that is.** The Policy Director answered it at [MOO-188](/MOO/issues/MOO-188) §3.3 with one wording change and commissioned the cut at §8; it is enacted in subdivision D 1 by store revision 65 and recorded at §0.0-65, §5.43 and §6.0-65 of [`bill-text-s8`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-bill-text-s8). **Answers (1), (2), (3), (5), (6) and (7) remain open and unanswered, and this marking decides none of them.**
    - **(1) F7 — subsection N fixes no time for payment, so whether a subsidy that buys no benefit comes back to the $3,000,000 cap is an unregulated administrative choice. Serious, and the reviewer's §4 shows it is the load-bearing fiscal assumption of the whole package.** Subdivision G 4 settles the subsidy *"on … the date of the member's death"*; subsection N says the Commonwealth shall pay *"upon the settlement"*; the release enacted in store revision 61 operates *"only to the extent the amount settled has not been paid."* So on subsection N's own words **nothing is ever released**, the cap carries a permanent charge for a benefit nobody received, and at the $170,000 per-election ceiling each such death **permanently removes one election from the admitted cohort — a 5.6 to 5.9 percent reduction in admissions per occurrence.** Two forms, alternatives. **(a) Bar the payment until the benefit is known**: *"The Commonwealth shall not pay an amount settled under subdivision G 4 on the death of a member before the Board has determined whether a retirement allowance is payable under § 51.1-162 on the member's death and, where such an allowance is payable, whether the person entitled to it elects under § 51.1-162 to be paid the member's accumulated contributions or a lump sum in lieu of it."* **(b) Repay and restore**: *"Where an amount settled under subdivision G 4 has been paid to the retirement system under this subsection and no retirement allowance is payable under § 51.1-162 on the member's death, the retirement system shall repay that amount to the Commonwealth no later than 60 days after the Board determines that no such allowance is payable, and the Board shall restore it to the amount available under the limitation stated in the first sentence of subdivision D 2."* **(a) delays the Commonwealth's cash and keeps the money out of the trust fund; (b) takes money back out of the trust fund after settlement, which is what [MOO-96](/MOO/issues/MOO-96)'s F-3 and Va. Const. art. X, § 11 bear on.** Routed to [@Fiscal & Impact Modeler](agent://428b53bf-f49e-4505-a607-3560a26b3d50) as the cohort question, not as a new figure.
    - **(2) The measure you approved has one incomputable side, and the cure is one clause.** f3-a compares the two benefits *"on the basis of actuarial equivalence as of the date of the member's death."* One side is the § 51.1-163 benefit, and § 51.1-163 measures *"any excess of his accumulated contributions **as of the effective date of his retirement**, over the total retirement allowances received by him"* — **in a pre-effective-date death that date never arrived.** Drafted, in the same sentence: *"and for the purposes of that comparison the benefit payable in accordance with § 51.1-163 shall be determined as though the effective date of the member's retirement were the date of death."* **It fixes an amount, which is why it is not enacted.** The alternative that dissolves the problem instead of solving it is **f3-c**, which you did not select: confine the greater-of to the case where § 51.1-165 C 2 itself pays under § 51.1-162 B, in which case the comparison is between two figures of the same kind. **f3-c takes a right from the designated beneficiary; answer (2) does not.**
