    - **(3) F5's discovery rule — the living member's 30 days run from a certification she cannot yet know is incomplete.** Store revision 62 removed the contradiction: there is now one window, and it runs from the certification she actually received. **It did not make the window useful to her**, which was the reviewer's point. Drafted, in subdivision D 1: *"or, where the person adversely affected is the member, no later than 30 days after the date on which the member files with the Board written evidence of the omission, whichever is later."* **It is an open-ended limitation period and that is a policy setting, not the resolution of a contradiction.**
    - **(4) F10's designation-review advisory — the one compelled statement that would make the purchase work for the member who is about to lose it.** § 51.1-162 B pays a **designated** spouse, minor child or parent. A member who designated her sister in 2009, buys 36 months for $26,000 with a $160,000 certified subsidy and is survived by her mother gets **nothing** from the purchase, and nothing in the act tells her to look. Store revision 62 corrects the proposition so it no longer misleads her; it does not advise her. Drafted, appended to that proposition: *"and that the member should review the member's designation of beneficiary under subsection A of § 51.1-162 before completing payment."* **It is a new compelled statement and was not agreed.** It is also, on the reviewer's analysis, the cheapest thing on this card. **ANSWERED AND ENACTED in store revision 65.** It was agreed by the Policy Director at [MOO-188](/MOO/issues/MOO-188) §3.3 and cut on [MOO-191](/MOO/issues/MOO-191) as one anchored substitution of 137 characters. **Two things changed on the way in.** The verb: the enacted words read *"that the member **is advised to** review the member's designation of beneficiary under subsection A of § 51.1-162 before completing payment"*, because every other item in that series is a proposition of law introduced by *"that"* and *"should review"* would have read as a statement of what the law requires of the member, which it is not. The place: **its own semicolon item immediately after the § 51.1-162 B proposition**, not an appendage to it, so the propositions of law stay together and the advice is visibly advice. **Do not re-cut it.** The consequence the Director took at MOO-188 §3.4 is that subdivision D 1's failure sentence reaches the advisory, so omitting it is reviewable under subsection M and reportable under subsection J; the advisory is not excepted from that sentence.
    - **(5) M7 — the agency death-notice duty closes on itself.** The Board must report every agency failure *"of which the Board becomes aware"*; if the agency never reports, the Board never learns of the death, so it never becomes aware of the failure, so nothing is reported. And the duty runs on *"the agency that employs or last employed"*, so a member who left state service for a private employer may have a last state agency that never hears she died. Drafted, in subdivision G 4: *"The Board shall, no less often than quarterly, compare the records of members whose elections it has accepted against the records of deaths maintained by the State Registrar of Vital Records and against any index of deaths maintained by an agency of the United States to which the Board has access, and the Board is deemed to have learned of a member's death on the date of a match."* **It is a new duty with an administrative cost, and the vital-records citation must come from [@Statutory Researcher](agent://d2a2feeb-989e-40ec-9cf6-496e7633daa6) before it is drafted into the act** — I have not cited a section I did not read.
    - **(6) M8 — the 120-day tolling cap binds the member and not the Board.** Subdivision M 2 caps aggregate tolling at 120 days *"excluding any period after the expiration of the 90 days allowed by this subsection and before the Board acts upon the request."* **Every day of Board delay past 90 days tolls without limit**, extending the member's final qualifying retirement date and keeping the certified subsidy charged against the cap for as long as the Board does not act. The reviewer is careful that this is **Board-operated, not a revival of the member's lever**, which [MOO-154](/MOO/issues/MOO-154)'s F16 declination correctly closed. Two forms, alternatives. **(a) Cap the excluded period**: *", provided that the aggregate of all periods so excluded shall not exceed 90 days"*. **(b) Restart the clock**: *"and the running of the member's final qualifying retirement date resumes upon the expiration of the 90 days allowed by this subsection notwithstanding that the Board has not acted upon the request."* **(b) is the stronger discipline and it can cost the member her qualifying retirement through no fault of her own; (a) cannot.**
    - **(7) The conforming amendment to § 51.1-162 C — and it changes the act's footprint.** f7-toll, enacted in this revision, provides that *"the period stated in subsection C of § 51.1-162 within which a waiver must be made does not expire before the thirtieth day after the notice is given."* **That is the act's first operative override of a period stated in a section this act does not amend**, and a reader of § 51.1-162 C will not learn of it from § 51.1-162 C. The conforming amendment is one clause — *"except as subdivision G 4 of § 51.1-142.4 provides"* — but drafting it **adds § 51.1-162 to the amended sections, changes the title line and changes the first enactment clause.** The act has amended one section and added one since revision 21 removed § 51.1-155 on your direction, and widening that is yours, not mine. **Not drafted.** Either way the override must travel in the drafting request to Division of Legislative Services, because a drafter's note is not enacted.
    - **One residual, flagged and not carded, because no finding reaches it.** The fiduciary cure enacted in this revision reaches every fiduciary the act writes to — all of them addressees of a **death** benefit. **A conservator acting for a living member under a disability receives the subdivision D 1 certification and is in no class in subdivision M 2.** The next counsel pass is asked to say whether that is a defect or a non-case. **ANSWERED — [MOO-195](/MOO/issues/MOO-195)'s pass 21, 12 October 2026: a non-case**, because subdivision M 2's class is a list of **parties in interest** and not of representatives, and a conservator appointed under Chapter 20 of Title 64.2 asserts no claim of their own — they exercise **the member's** standing, and *"a member"* is the first category M 2 names. The act's other fiduciary references are in subdivision G 4 and are needed there precisely because they concern **death** benefits, where the member has no standing left to exercise. **The narrow residual the pass does leave is carded as item 134 and is drafted and not enacted.**



    **ANSWERS (1), (2), (6) AND (7) ARE ANSWERED AND ENACTED — store revision 67, 12 October 2026.** The Policy Director approved **f7-a**, **s163-valuation**, **m8-a** and **s162c-conforming** on the pending card at **18:04:59 UTC on 12 October 2026**; all four are in subsection N, subdivision G 4, subdivision M 2 and — for the conforming amendment — **the title line, the first enactment clause and § 51.1-162 reproduced in full as amended**. Recorded at §0.0-67, §5.45 and §6.0-67 of [`bill-text-s8`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-bill-text-s8) and at [`moo189-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-97#document-moo189-disposition). **f7-b, f3-c-instead, f5-discovery, m7-matching and m8-b were not selected**; f5-discovery and m7-matching stay open on this item and the three alternates are closed by the selection of their alternatives. **One word of f7-a changed on the way in**: *"in lieu of **it**"* became *"in lieu of **any other benefit under that section**"*, which is § 51.1-162 B's own phrase and the phrase subdivision G 4 already used twice — §6.0-67 item 3. **Answer (4) remains answered and enacted in store revision 65. Answers (3) and (5) are the only ones still open, and this marking decides neither.**
130. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 63, out of enacting the board's answer to item 124. It is not a defect in the answer; it is the thing the answer deliberately does not touch, and it belongs to two other owners.** The enacted denial is **not** deemed to be the Board *"acting upon the request"*, and I would not make it so by drafting, because that verb is the subject of [MOO-164](/MOO/issues/MOO-164) and because deeming it would have moved two endpoints in passing. **The consequence, on the text as it now stands:** a Board that never acts leaves (i) the tolling of the member's final qualifying retirement date and of every period within which she must act, and (ii) the subdivision D 4 / D 5 **filing-date retention** — which is [MOO-141](/MOO/issues/MOO-141) card (k)'s endpoint, and can move an applicant from the subsidised cost to the standard cost under subsection B of § 51.1-142.2 — **running without end**, because both endpoints are the Board's act and M 2 clause (ii) expressly excludes from its 120-day aggregate *"any period after the expiration of the 90 days allowed by this subsection and before the Board acts upon the request."* **The cure is already enacted in this bill for the mirror-image case:** store revision 62's subdivision G 4 sentence *"a failure of the Board to act upon such a request within the period allowed to it for acting upon the request shall not extend that aggregate."* **Three routes, none enacted:** **(a)** nothing — item 124's answer gives her a decision, and indefinite tolling of her own deadline is favourable to her on the deadline and adverse on the zero-allowance window, which is the trade [MOO-141](/MOO/issues/MOO-141) §5.2 prices; **(b)** give M 2 clause (ii) G 4's words, which caps the tolling and is the smallest change — **I recommend this one, and it is [MOO-97](/MOO/issues/MOO-97)'s uncapped-tolling residual rather than mine to take**; **(c)** deem the denial an act upon the request for the purposes of the two endpoints only, which answers both at once and **pre-empts MOO-164**, so I will not draft it until MOO-164 lands. **No figure moves on (a) or (c); (b) moves a date and therefore may move a figure, which is why it is routed and not taken.**


    **ANNOTATION, store revision 69 — route (b) is DECLINED by the Policy Director, as a decision and not by implication, and so is (b) + (c). This annotation REPLACES store revision 67's, which recorded the recommendation as *held*: a record that says *held* against a decision that says *declined* is a defect of the record.** The declination is of 13 October 2026, out of [MOO-164](/MOO/issues/MOO-164), with the reasoning at [`moo164-policy-director-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-164#document-moo164-policy-director-disposition) §6.

    - **The ground is incidence.** Subdivision G 4's *"a failure of the Board to act upon such a request … shall not extend that aggregate"* caps **the Board's own** clocks, so there those words make the Board bear its own delay. Subdivision M 2 clause (ii)'s aggregate caps **the member's** tolling, so the identical words make the **member** bear it. **The form is conserved and the effect reverses.** Route (b) must never be drafted on any issue or in any store revision, and **must never be written up as a conforming amendment.** The standing statement is [`standing-drafting-directions`](/MOO/issues/MOO-97#document-standing-drafting-directions) Part A5.
    - **Route (b) also buys less than this item's own table said, and the Director's note corrects it.** The subdivision D 4 / D 5 filing-date retention and the 15-day duty to act on the application are **one sentence, 112 characters apart, and it is the sentence *after* clause (ii)** — so **no amendment confined to clause (ii) reaches either limb on any reading.** Route (b) could only ever have bought limb (i), and limb (i) is already bounded: **m8-a**, enacted at store revision 67, closes clause (ii) with *"provided that the aggregate of all periods so excluded shall not exceed 90 days"*, so the member's tolling is bounded at 120 days plus at most 90 instead of running without end, **and the incidence is not reversed — she can never lose her qualifying retirement through the Board's delay.**
    - **Route (c) is adopted instead**, in [MOO-164](/MOO/issues/MOO-164): candidate 164-7 with the trigger limb (B) that revision 5 found had been dropped. It reaches all three remaining limbs and moves no figure. **Why not (b) + (c), which was the real question:** clause (ii) would be bounded twice, by two instruments for one concept, the second drafted as an exception to the first — and the only case (b) uniquely covers is the case where unbounded tolling **favours** the member, whose floor MOO-164's Edit 164-4 already protects without any filing by anyone.
    - **Limb (iv) is answered as to the date and not as to the remedy.** Candidate 164-7-B is taken, so the request class under the definition of *"final qualifying retirement date"* in subsection A gets a determinate date too. **It still has no deemed denial and therefore no case decision**, where the reconsideration class has one. The Director has carded that asymmetry and **it is being drafted nowhere.**
    - **[MOO-92](/MOO/issues/MOO-92)'s third residual — M 2's uncapped tolling, which is what this item is — is closed by this decision.** Nothing in it is open to the Drafting Specialist and nothing is drafted for it. **One mechanical consequence for anyone holding a cut against this clause: a FIND ending *"...before the Board acts upon the request."* with a trailing full stop no longer resolves**, because m8-a appends to that sentence. Re-derive it against the live body.
131. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 63 — and it is a defect in the two sentences I drafted, not in the board's answer to item 124.** Found on re-reading the enacted text in its live context. The denial is characterised as *"the final determination of the Board upon the reconsideration and a case decision as defined in § 2.2-4001 **for the purposes of this subsection**."* **Two readings interlock: each is survivable alone, and the pair closes the route into court that the cure exists to open.**
    - **(a) The express Article 5 grant may not reach her.** Subdivision M 2's grant runs to a person *"aggrieved by, and claiming the unlawfulness of, **such case decision**"*, and that sentence stands immediately after *"Upon completion of a reconsideration under this subsection, the final determination issued by the Board shall constitute a case decision as defined in § 2.2-4001."* On the nearest-antecedent reading *"such case decision"* is the **completed** reconsideration's determination — and a deemed denial is, by definition, not a completed one. **Repair, one phrase, drafted and not enacted:** for *"such case decision"* read <u>*"a case decision described in this subsection"*</u>.
    - **(b) My own qualifying phrase may switch the characterisation off exactly where she needs it.** If (a) goes against her she must fall back on M 2's savings sentence — *"Nothing in this subsection limits the right of any person under § 2.2-4026 to seek judicial review of a case decision"* — and **§ 2.2-4026 sits outside this subsection**, which is the one place *"for the purposes of this subsection"* arguably does not reach. **Repair, five words out, drafted and not enacted:** delete <u>*"for the purposes of this subsection"*</u> from the enacted sentence. That is also what the model does: **§ 9.1-405** and M 2's own completed-reconsideration sentence each characterise a determination as a case decision with **no such limit**.
    - **Why neither is enacted.** The phrase is in the option (d) text the board approved **verbatim** at 17:38 UTC on 12 October 2026, so cutting it changes approved words; and (a)'s repair edits a sentence [MOO-169](/MOO/issues/MOO-169)'s counsel pass certified. **Either repair alone closes the pair. (b) is the one I would take**, because it makes the sentence match its own model and leaves the certified sentence untouched. **No figure moves on either, no designator is renumbered, and nobody is admitted or refused by either.**
    - **One thing I did not verify and am not asserting:** the operative words of § 2.2-4026 subsection A. The finding does not depend on them — it is a scope mismatch on the face of our own text — but whether the savings sentence is a **sufficient** route turns on them. Named to [MOO-190](/MOO/issues/MOO-190) as the first thing to break, and the research question is with [MOO-186](/MOO/issues/MOO-186).


132. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 67, out of [MOO-189](/MOO/issues/MOO-189)'s counsel pass on store revision 62 — six answers drafted in full and enacted nowhere. Three of them decide who is paid and I have picked none.** Eleven of that pass's findings are enacted in store revision 67 as conformance of the act to its own cited sections; these six are not, because each either resolves a **fatal** finding, decides who bears a loss, or is a policy setting the reviewer expressly left open. Every word below is drafted. Full disposition finding by finding: [`moo189-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-97#document-moo189-disposition).
    - **(1) F1 — FATAL. The death branch does not reach the member who dies with a disability retirement pending and whose effective date the Board sets, after her death, to a date before it.** A disability retirement is not a *"qualifying retirement"*, so clause (i)'s own carve-out — *"does not apply where the member has retired in a retirement that is not a qualifying retirement before the date of death"* — switches clause (i) off, and the death branch is conditioned on clause (i) applying. **This is not an edge case:** § 51.1-156 D allows an effective date up to ninety days before the notice of retirement is filed and lets the Board waive even that for good cause, § 51.1-156 F lets the appointing authority file for an incapacitated member, and subdivision G 4 already drafts around post-death approval. On the reviewer's facts the widower receives § 51.1-163's refund of about $92,600 instead of a § 51.1-162 B allowance worth about $260,000 — **a loss of about $167,000 in present value, decided by an administrative date chosen after his wife was dead, under no standard the act states.** Clause (ii) still saves the service credit, so [MOO-161](/MOO/issues/MOO-161) F1's forfeiture does not return; the loss is the death benefit. **Three forms, alternatives.** **(a) Commenced in payment** — for the carve-out read *"does not apply where, before the date of death, the member has retired in a retirement that is not a qualifying retirement and a retirement allowance has become payable to the member under that retirement"*, and conform the branch's own condition to *"no retirement allowance has become payable to the member before the date of death"*. **It leaves the back-dated case arguable**, because an allowance may be said to have become payable from a date the Board set after the death. **(b) The date of determination — the reviewer's own words** — add to subdivision G 4: *"except that a retirement the effective date of which the Board determines after the date of the member's death is not a retirement before the date of death for the purposes of this subdivision"*. **It is the only form that reaches the class cleanly, and it keys on an act of the Board**, which is the family of condition your standing direction of 12 October forecloses for this sentence — **which is exactly why it is here and not in the text.** **(c) The greater of the two** — leave the carve-out alone and provide that where the member dies before a retirement allowance is actually paid to her, the Board shall pay the greater of the benefit § 51.1-163 provides and the benefit subdivision G 4 would provide, to the payee of the greater. **(c) is the only one of the three that cannot make any person worse off than present law**, and it costs the trust fund the difference. **Rated fatal by counsel. The defect is in the pre-existing carve-out and not in store revision 62's words, and resolving a fatal finding by drafting choice is the one thing this role may not do.**
    - **(2) F4 — subdivision G 4's savings clause and its greater-of cannot both stand, and where they conflict a named person loses everything rather than the difference.** The sentence opens *"Nothing in the preceding sentence reduces a benefit payable under subdivision C 2 of § 51.1-165"* and closes by directing the Board to pay the greater of two benefits **to the payee of the greater**. § 51.1-165 C 2's exception fires only where the contingent annuitant and the § 51.1-162 B payee are the same person; where they are not, C 2 pays under § 51.1-163 to the designated beneficiary. On the reviewer's facts the brother, named contingent annuitant and designated beneficiary, loses the whole **$94,000** while the surviving husband takes $260,000 — and the savings clause says his benefit is not reduced. **Three forms, alternatives.** **(a) Confine the greater-of to coincident payees**, leaving the non-coincident case entirely to § 51.1-165 C 2 — the brother keeps his $94,000 and the husband gets nothing from the act. **(b) Pay both** — the greater to its payee and the lesser provision's payee what that provision would have given him; it costs the trust fund both amounts and is the only form in which nobody loses. **(c) Delete the savings clause** — strike *"Nothing in the preceding sentence reduces a benefit payable under subdivision C 2 of § 51.1-165, and"*; the brother loses $94,000 and the text stops saying otherwise. **I will not pick one. The sentence is internally contradictory and every resolution of it decides who is paid.**
    - **(3) F12 — the § 51.1-162 C waiver floor protects far less than its words suggest, and the reviewer left the measure to you in terms.** f7-toll provides that the ninety days do not expire *"before the thirtieth day after the notice is given."* **The floor therefore does nothing unless the notice is given after day 60**, and a beneficiary noticed on day 85 gets thirty days where a timely-noticed beneficiary got ninety. Drafted: for *"thirtieth"* read <u>*"ninetieth"*</u>. **It gives the survivor the Code's own measure from the Board's own notice rather than a floor**, and it lengthens the period in which a § 51.1-162 B allowance can be made available to somebody else, which is a cost to the person holding the subsection C allowance.
    - **(4) F3's operative half — the certification is now true, and the act still does not enact the proposition.** Store revision 67 corrects the certification: it no longer says that no option elected under § 51.1-165 governs the death benefit, and it names the one that may — the straight life option under subdivision A 1, an election *"to receive an increased retirement allowance in lieu of any death benefits."* **So a member who elected it, filed the § 51.1-153 notice subdivision D 1 compels her to file, and died before her effective date may have no death benefit at all**, and the Commonwealth's subsidy will have bought her family nothing. Drafted, in subdivision G 4: *"and no option elected under § 51.1-165 or § 51.1-165.01 limits or displaces a benefit determined under this subdivision."* **It takes from the member the bargain she was paid for** — she received a larger allowance in her own lifetime in exchange for the death benefit — and it pays her survivors out of the trust fund instead. **Verified at source on 12 October 2026 and narrower than the finding:** subdivision A 2 carries its own carve-out for a pre-effective-date death, A 3 says nothing about death benefits, and no option in § 51.1-165.01 extinguishes one. **Subdivision A 1 is the whole of the exposure.**
    - **(5) F14's second half — a state-system eligibility bar sits inside the local-mandate disclaimer, where nobody will look for it.** Subsection L opens *"Nothing in this section imposes any duty or cost upon a county, city, or town"*, and your answer **f10-b** put the Chapter 2, 2.1 and 3 exclusion there. Store revision 67 cured the half that was a defect — the enumeration was **partial**, naming five of the definition's eight exclusions, and it now cites the definition instead. **The placement is not a defect and I have not moved it**: moving it to subsection B, where *"An eligible member may purchase"* lives, is one substitution and it is a presentational choice about words you approved.
    - **(6) F11's authority, which is a request and not a drafting answer.** The reviewer reports the hidden-override risk as **constitutionally sound** — Article IV, Section 12 of the Constitution of Virginia requires reenactment of an *amended* section, and an act complete in itself that supersedes prior law by implication is not an amendment in that sense — **with one express limit: the reviewer verified the constitutional text but did not locate controlling Supreme Court of Virginia authority on the "complete in itself" doctrine within the pass, and offered to run it down.** The conforming amendment enacted in store revision 67 removes the risk for § 51.1-162 C. **It does not remove it for the two *"notwithstanding"* displacements in subdivision G 4**, which reach § 51.1-165 C 1 and § 51.1-165.01 F and are not accompanied by any amendment to either. Say whether you want that authority hardened before the drafting request goes to Division of Legislative Services.

    **ANNOTATION, store revision 69 — THE CARD IS ANSWERED AND THREE OF THE FOUR ANSWERS ARE IN THE TEXT.** The Policy Director accepted **f1-b**, **f4-a**, **f14-move** and **f11-authority** at **18:35:16 UTC on 12 October 2026** (card `829fa6d2`). Store revision 69 enacts the three that are drafting, in five substitutions: subdivision G 4 (three) and subsections B and L (one each). Record: §0.0-69, sweep §5.46, drafter's note §6.0-69 of [`bill-text-s8`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-bill-text-s8).

    - **f1-b — enacted, with the Director's words reproduced verbatim and two conforming limbs added, each recorded at §6.0-69 items 2 and 3 and each reversible in one substitution.** (i) *"that is not a qualifying retirement"*, without which the exception — confined only by *"for the purposes of this subdivision"* — reached **a member who retired in a qualifying retirement** and pulled a retiree out of § 51.1-163, which is the class [MOO-161](/MOO/issues/MOO-161)'s F1 rated fatal and the class the standing direction protects. (ii) *"and for those purposes the member shall not be treated as having retired before the date of death in such a retirement"*, because the approved words define the **noun** *"a retirement before the date of death"* while the branch's own condition is the **verb** *"the member has not retired"* — without the limb **the approved answer did not reach the class it was approved to reach.**
    - **One residual of f1-b declined with its reason, and it is the first thing the next pass is told to attack.** A disability retiree **already in payment** whose effective date the Board *re-determines* after her death is, on the words, a member whose effective date the Board determined after the death. **f1-a's limb would close it and f1-a was not selected**, so taking it now would be re-deciding the card.
    - **f4-a — enacted. The card described option (a) and did not draft it, so the words are mine and the policy is the Director's.** The greater-of now runs only where subdivision C 2 of § 51.1-165 and the § 51.1-162 determination are payable to the same person or persons; where they are not, **the C 2 benefit is paid and the benefit determined under the preceding sentence is not.** The branch is a semicolon clause and **not a sentence of its own, because that sentence now carries five references to *"the preceding sentence"* and all five mean one sentence.** Consequence, stated because it decides who is paid: where C 2's own switch to § 51.1-162 B fails, C 2 requires payment under § 51.1-163, so the family takes a **refund** where store revisions 67 and 68 would have paid an **allowance** — which is what Chapter 1 does for that family today. **No person is worse off than under present law and the act buys that class nothing.**
    - **f14-move — enacted, word for word, net zero characters.** The bar leaves subsection L and stands in subsection B after the sentence that makes an accepted election the only route to a purchase. *"Subsection L"* is cited nowhere in the act, so nothing strands, and no citation count changes.
    - **f11-authority — commissioned as [MOO-207](/MOO/issues/MOO-207)**, assigned, self-contained, and **not drafting**: the *"complete in itself"* authority under Va. Const. art. IV, § 12 for subdivision G 4's two *"notwithstanding"* displacements, which reach subdivision C 1 of § 51.1-165 and subsection F of § 51.1-165.01 and are accompanied by no amendment to either.
    - **Closed by the selection of their alternatives, and kept rather than deleted: f1-a, f1-c, f4-b and f4-c.** **Still open and still drafted word for word: (3) f12-ninety** — the § 51.1-162 C waiver floor stays at the thirtieth day after the notice — **and (4) f3-operative**, so a member who elected the straight life option under subdivision A 1 of § 51.1-165 may still have no death benefit for the subsidy to buy. **This annotation decides neither.**
    - **[MOO-200](/MOO/issues/MOO-200)'s pass landed at 00:15 UTC on 13 October 2026, after this card was answered, and three of its findings bear on these answers.** **F14** is *aggravated* by f14-move: subsection L exists to tell localities and school boards that the act does not reach them, and the subsection addressed to them now says nothing on the subject — and F14's separate point, that the bar has **no time anchor** while subdivision D 4 determines eligibility as of the filing date of the complete application, survives the move unchanged. **F6 and F7** attack the sentence f4-a amends — F7 that the greater-of comparison *"changes no amount for anyone"*, F6 that the named assumption set is not identifiable because § 51.1-124.3 defines *"actuarial equivalent"* by *"actuarial tables **adopted** by the Board"* — and **f4-a neither cures nor worsens either.** **MOO-200's eighteen findings are not disposed in store revision 69; that is the next revision.**


133. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 68, out of [MOO-195](/MOO/issues/MOO-195)'s pass 21 on the designation-review advisory (finding F7) — drafted in full and enacted nowhere. RE-SCOPED IN STORE REVISION 70 on your direction at [MOO-208](/MOO/issues/MOO-208), F3 having been taken. The failure sentence catches omission and misses misstatement.** Subdivision D 1's failure sentence reaches *"a **failure to state** in the Board's certification every matter this subdivision requires to be stated in it"*, and subdivision M 2's general limb reaches *"a **failure** of the Board to state in a certification or notice a matter this section requires to be stated in it."* **Both are framed on absence. Neither reaches a matter that is stated and wrong.** 

    **CORRECTED IN STORE REVISION 72** — [MOO-211](/MOO/issues/MOO-211) Finding 2: **that premise is overstated, and narrowing it lowers this card's priority.** **Most misstatements decompose into omissions**, because each required matter is required *with its qualifiers*. The required matter here is *"that the Board advises the member to review the member's designation of beneficiary"* — and **this card's own worked example**, a template rewritten to *"Your beneficiary designation on file with VRS will apply to this purchase,"* **does not state that matter at all.** It is an omission; it is inside D 1's failure sentence and inside M 2's limb; and this card used it to prove a gap it does not fall into. The same holds for the propositions of law: dropping *"only to a surviving spouse, minor child, or parent"* from the § 51.1-162 B proposition is a **failure to state the required matter**, not a misstatement outside it. **The real gap is additive misstatement only** — the Board states the required matter correctly and adds something false beside it (*"and your designation will lapse if you do not act before completing payment"*). That is **narrower than this card describes and rarer than a template rewrite**, and it is worth knowing before 253 characters are spent on it.

    **Why it bites hardest on the advisory, which is why the pass raised it here.** The six propositions of law in D 1's series are long and interlocking and a certification template will lift them as one block from the statute; they are present or absent together. **The advisory is short, separable and in a different register, and it is exactly the line a plain-language template editor rewrites.** Rendered as *"Your beneficiary designation on file with VRS will apply to this purchase,"* it is present — so it is no omission — and it is **wrong**, because the designation on file may defeat the subsection B allowance entirely. The member is actively misled, there is no channel, and the subsection J report records the matter as stated.

    **Drafted as it stood at store revision 68, before F3 was taken:** *"A failure to state in the Board's certification every matter this subdivision requires to be stated in it<u>, or the statement of any such matter otherwise than as this subdivision requires it to be stated,</u> is a failure of the Board…"* **That scope is now wrong**, because *"any such matter"* reaches the advice, which store revision 70 has excepted from D 1's subsection M deeming. A misstatement limb over the advice would re-open the channel you closed.

    **Re-scoped, store revision 70. This is the drafted text as it now stands, against the failure sentence as store revision 70 left it:** *"A failure to state in the Board's certification every matter this subdivision requires to be stated in it, other than a failure to state that the Board advises the member to review the member's designation of beneficiary, <u>or the statement in the Board's certification of any matter this subdivision requires to be stated in it, other than that the Board advises the member to review the member's designation of beneficiary, otherwise than as this subdivision requires it to be stated,</u> is a failure of the Board to state in a certification a matter this section requires to be stated in it for the purposes of subsection M…"* **+253 characters.** The exception is written in the same words the enacted exception uses, so the two limbs share one vocabulary and the phrase does one job in both.

    **What the re-scoping reaches, and where it differs from your instruction. Recorded, not drafted around.** You directed that the limb reach *"the six propositions of law and not the advisory."* **The drafted words reach every required matter other than the advice — the six propositions *and* item 7's other two propositions, the power to re-designate and the no-fault clause.** That is the F3 residue you named at §3 of the disposition, surfacing a second time: D3c's exception names only the first of item 7's three propositions, so those two stay inside D 1's deeming for omission, and the limb above keeps them inside it for misstatement too. **Scoping strictly to the six and no further is not draftable on the present text**: the seven items are undesignated clauses of one sentence, there is no handle to cite, and the only clean handle is designating them (i) through (vii) — a renumbering your commission forbade and a separate edit. ~~**The alternative, if you want the strict scope, is that renumbering**; it is one cut over one sentence and it would also give the M exception a handle.~~ §6.0-70 item 6. 

    **CORRECTED IN STORE REVISION 72** — [MOO-211](/MOO/issues/MOO-211) M1: **there is nothing to scope around and no renumbering is needed.** After F4 and F1/F2/F6/F9, **item 7 carries one advisory and two propositions of law** — the power to re-designate and the no-fault clause — so **the certification series carries eight propositions of law and one advisory, not six and one.** The instruction *"the six propositions of law and not the advisory"* was therefore **under-inclusive by two**, and the drafted scope *"every matter other than the advice"* reaches **all eight** and excepts the one — **which is exactly what the instruction intended.** The asymmetry recorded above was an artefact of the miscount and does not arise on the drafted words. **The (i)–(vii) renumbering your commission forbade is not needed for anything, and the three-way split is a solution to a problem that does not exist.** The drafted words stand unchanged at +253.

    **Still not enacted, and the reason has changed.** At store revision 68 the third and controlling reason was *"decide F3 first."* **F3 is now decided and taken**, so that reason is spent. What remains: the defect is a property of a sentence **you approved at store revision 62** and not of the advisory, you commissioned store revision 70 as six named substitutions and this is not one of them, and the strict scope you asked for needs a renumbering you forbade. **So the words are re-scoped and the card stays open**, as you directed at [MOO-208](/MOO/issues/MOO-208). The whole disposition is at [`moo191-director-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-191#document-moo191-director-disposition).

    **One thing store revision 70 changed about this item's premise, and it cuts the other way.** This item has always said of the advisory that *"there is no channel."* **After store revision 70 there still is one** — subdivision M 2 reaches a failure to state a matter this section requires to be stated on its own terms, and D 1's exception withdraws only D 1's own extension of it. That is **§7 item 135**, and it means the misstatement gap this item describes sits next to an omission channel that F3 did not in fact close. **Read items 133 and 135 together before deciding either.**

134. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 68 — item 129's conservator residual, answered. A non-case on the main question, with one narrow intake residual drafted and not enacted.** Item 129 asked the next counsel pass whether it is a defect that a conservator acting for a **living** member under a disability receives the subdivision D 1 certification and appears in no class in subdivision M 2. [MOO-195](/MOO/issues/MOO-195)'s pass 21 §7 answers: **a non-case.** M 2's class is a list of **parties in interest**, not of representatives. A conservator appointed under Chapter 20 of Title 64.2 asserts no claim of their own; they exercise **the member's** standing, and *"a member"* is the first category M 2 names. The act's other fiduciary references sit in subdivision G 4 and are needed there because those provisions concern **death** benefits, where the member has no standing left to exercise and the fiduciary must be named in their own right.

    **The residual, which is narrow and real enough to card.** Subdivision M 2 opens *"A member … adversely affected by a determination"* and then requires a *"written request filed."* **If the Board reads the filing requirement as personal to the member, a conservator's filing could be refused at intake**, forcing a petition that would plainly succeed and costing the member the time the tolling provision exists to give her.

    **Drafted, as one clause added to M 2's class:** *"<u>or a fiduciary within the meaning of § 51.1-1012 acting for any of them</u>."*

    **Not enacted.** It is outside the scope of the one-edit cut at [MOO-191](/MOO/issues/MOO-191), it widens a standing class you have not been asked about, and the answer above is enough to close the residual as item 129 posed it. **Say whether you want the clause; it is one substitution and it reverses nothing.**

135. ****CLOSED IN STORE REVISION 73** — you approved form M-a at [MOO-208](/MOO/issues/MOO-208) and it is enacted as S1, +115, in subsection M 2. NEW IN STORE REVISION 70, out of the cross-reference sweep for that revision (§5.47 direction 2, check 1); drafted in two forms, one enacted. F3's exception did not close the subsection M channel, because subdivision M 2 does not look at subdivision D 1 — and the cure does not close it either, for a reason this item did not reach. See item 137.** You took [MOO-195](/MOO/issues/MOO-195) F3 in form D3c conformed at [MOO-208](/MOO/issues/MOO-208), to except omission of the designation-review advice from subsection M review while keeping it in the subsection J report. **The J half works in full.** The M half does not, and the reason is in the act's own words.

    **Subdivision M 2's first sentence confers the channel independently.** It gives written reconsideration to a person adversely affected *"by **a failure of the Board to state in a certification or notice a matter this section requires to be stated in it**."* The advice is a matter **this subdivision** requires to be stated and D 1 is part of **this section**, so the omission is inside M 2's limb with no help from D 1.

    **D 1's sentence extends M 2; it does not constitute it.** M 2's own clock for a failure to state runs *"within 30 days after the certification or notice is given or, **where the provision that requires the matter to be stated provides a longer or later period, within that period**."* M 2 therefore anticipates that a provision like D 1 will extend it — which is precisely what D 1's bespoke clock does, giving the member 30 days from the certification and a claimant who is not the member 30 days from the subdivision G 4 notice. **An exception written into a conferring sentence confers less; it does not withdraw what another subdivision independently gives.** And §1 carries six *"for the purposes of subsection M"* clauses, the other five all declaratory (*"X is a determination of the Board for the purposes of subsection M"*), while M 2's second sentence says in terms that its own reach operates *"whether or not another provision of this section expressly provides for review of it."*

    **So what the exception actually did.** The member keeps reconsideration under M 2 on a 30-day clock from the certification. A claimant who is not the member — a survivor — **loses the subdivision G 4-notice clock, which was the only clock she could ever have used**, because the certification is handed to the member years before the survivor learns the designation was wrong. And M 2's second sentence, which D 1 had disapplied for the member, applies to her case again. **That is narrower than the status quo for the survivor and unchanged for the member, which is close to the opposite of the trade you took F3 for.**

    **No cure inside D 1 can work**, because the defect is that M 2 does not look at D 1. The clause has to go in M 2.

    ****CORRECTED IN STORE REVISION 72** — [MOO-211](/MOO/issues/MOO-211) agreed this item and added the argument that settles it, plus a second harm this item had missed.**

    **The argument that settles it: D 1 expressly disapplies M 2's second sentence, and you cannot disapply a provision that does not apply of its own force.** The disapplication is intelligible only on the premise that M 2 reaches the failure independently of D 1, so **the drafter's own words foreclose the contrary reading.** The contrary reading at its best is not *expressio unius* but the specific governing the general (*Dodson v. Potomac Mack Sales & Serv.*, 241 Va. 89 (1991)) with the presumption against surplusage (*Hubbard v. Henrico Ltd. P'ship*, 255 Va. 335 (1998)), on which the exception must do something and the only thing left is to make D 1 exhaustive. It loses structurally: **D 1 can add to subsection M by defining; it cannot subtract from subsection M except by amending subsection M.** The exception is not nugatory — it withdraws D 1's clock and D 1's disapplication — but it is in the wrong provision to do the job it was taken for, and the contrary reading is strong enough to litigate and not strong enough to rely on.

    **The second harm, and it inverts the subdivision.** By carving the advice failure out of the **whole** sentence, D3c also carved it out of *"the second sentence of subdivision M 2 does not apply to such a failure where the person adversely affected is the member."* So **for the advice failure M 2's second sentence applies in full**: the certification is itself *"a determination of the Board for the purposes of this subsection, whether or not another provision of this section expressly provides for review of it."* A member whose certification omits the advice can plead the certification as a reviewable determination — **a channel the member whose certification omits one of the propositions of law does not have**, because for her the second sentence is disapplied. **The member whose certification omits the least legally substantive of the seven items now has the widest channel of any member in the subdivision.** That is the opposite of F3's purpose and it is a second, independent reason the clause has to move to M 2. **Two forms, both +1 substitution, neither enacted.**

    **Form M-a — name the matter in M 2, in the words the act already uses. Recommended.** FIND, 128 characters, 1× in §1: *"or by a failure of the Board to state in a certification or notice a matter this section requires to be stated in it, may obtain"*. READ, as this item first wrote it and **struck through here so that it cannot be lifted from this card by mistake**: ~~*"…requires to be stated in it<u>, other than a failure to state that the Board advises the member to review the member's designation of beneficiary</u>, may obtain"* — +116 characters~~. **CORRECTED IN STORE REVISION 72**, on [MOO-211](/MOO/issues/MOO-211) m1 and on a markup check that pass did not make. **Two things in that READ string were wrong and the second would have damaged the text.**

    **The markup. Subsection M 2 is wholly new language and the insertion point sits inside an open `<u>` span**, so the tags written above would have **nested**, taking §1 from 62/62 to 63/63 and breaking the standing post-condition on the tag counts. **The cure carries no tags.** READ, corrected: *"or by a failure of the Board to state in a certification or notice a matter this section requires to be stated in it, other than a failure to state that the Board advises the member to review the member's designation of beneficiary, may obtain"*.

    **The arithmetic. +115, not +116** — the FIND already ends *"in it, may obtain"*, so the existing comma is retained and the insertion is the 115-character form without a trailing comma, not the 116-character form substitution 4 used in subdivision D 1. **+115 characters, no markup, and no conforming change anywhere else.** Re-resolved on store revision 71: the FIND still resolves **1× inside the §1 slice**, and the cure leaves `<u>`/`</u>` at **62 / 62**. The exception then appears a fourth time in the same words, so there is still one vocabulary and one concept; it is visible in the provision that confers the channel, which is where a reader of subsection M looks; and it needs no reader to treat D 1's deeming clause as exhaustive.

    **Form M-b — a generic hook in M 2, plus a conforming clause in D 1.** READ in M 2: *"…a matter this section requires to be stated in it, other than a matter that the provision of this section requiring it to be stated expressly excepts from this subsection, may obtain"* — ~~+101 characters~~ **+121 characters**, **CORRECTED IN STORE REVISION 72** on [MOO-211](/MOO/issues/MOO-211) m1: the figure was out by 20 and neither this item nor that pass could reconstruct the arithmetic that produced 101, and **the tags are dropped for the same reason as M-a**. The **+42 in D 1** this form also needs has **no drafted words in this card and remains unpriced**, so M-b's true cost is +121 plus an undrafted edit, against M-a's +115 and nothing. **A second reason to prefer M-a**, beyond the one below: M-b creates a standing invitation, because after it any future exception written anywhere in the section silently withdraws subsection M review with no reader of subsection M able to see which matters are excepted without auditing the whole section, using the same hook M 2's clock already uses (*"the provision that requires the matter to be stated"*). It is cheaper for a second exception later, but it needs **+42 in D 1** so that D 1's exception says expressly that it excepts the matter from subsection M, and it buys a cross-reference by description where M-a buys one by name. **Take M-b only if you expect more exceptions.**

    **What neither form does — and **CORRECTED IN STORE REVISION 72**: this is not a residue and there is no third decision.** Neither form reaches item 7's other two propositions, the power to re-designate and the no-fault clause, because D3c's exception does not. **This item recorded that as a gap to be closed. It is not a gap: it is the right answer.** Those two clauses are **propositions of law**, not advice — [MOO-211](/MOO/issues/MOO-211) M1 establishes that item 7 carries one advisory and two propositions of law, so the certification series carries **eight propositions of law and one advisory**, not six and one. **The whole point of F3 was to except the advice from subsection M review, and the whole point of leaving the propositions of law alone is that a member or survivor should keep review of a misstated or omitted proposition of law.** Widening the exception to all three would **remove review of two propositions of law that nobody proposed to remove it from**. **So the exception correctly names the advice and only the advice, in both D 1 as enacted and in either form of this cure.** *(This extension of M1 to this item is the drafter's and not counsel's; counsel made the point against §7 item 133. If you read it the other way, say so and it is one more substitution — but it is not a defect to be fixed silently.)*

    ~~**Why it is not cut.** It opens subdivision M 2, which store revision 70's commission did not open; it reverses nothing you decided, but it changes the effect of something you did decide, and that is yours and not mine.~~ **The whole of the reasoning is at §6.0-70 item 5 and §5.47 direction 2, check 1.** Read with item 133.

    ****CLOSED IN STORE REVISION 73**. What was cut.** Form **M-a**, at **+115 and no markup**, in subsection M 2's first sentence: *"…a matter this section requires to be stated in it**, other than a failure to state that the Board advises the member to review the member's designation of beneficiary**, may obtain…"*. The anchor resolved **1× inside the §1 slice** at the moment it was applied and the cure left `<u>`/`</u>` at **62 / 62**, subsection M 2 being wholly new language so that a tag would have nested. **Form M-b is not cut and is now spent.** The conformed phrase stands at **4× in §1**, byte-identical in every place — item 7, subdivision D 1's exception, D 1's subsection J limb and now M 2's exception.

    **And the thing this item did not reach, which is why it closes into item 137.** M 2's **second** sentence deems *"Every determination, **certification**, redetermination, …"* a determination of the Board for the purposes of the subsection. **M-a excepts the advice failure from the failure-to-state limb and leaves the determination limb untouched**, so the same member can bring the same grievance as a person *"adversely affected by a determination of the Board under this section."* This item argued that the channel was open because M 2 does not look at D 1; **it is also open because M 2 looks at the certification itself.** Item 137 carries the cure.

136. **Sixteen drafted answers out of [MOO-210](/MOO/issues/MOO-210) and [MOO-200](/MOO/issues/MOO-200), none enacted — and the first of them answers a FATAL finding.** Written in store revision 71 by the Drafting Specialist; every word of every answer is at [`moo210-moo200-disposition`](/MOO/issues/MOO-97#document-moo210-moo200-disposition). **This item replaces the card pending on [MOO-97](/MOO/issues/MOO-97) since store revision 69, whose options (4) and (5) were written on MOO-200 F7's premise that the greater-of comparison *"changes no amount for anyone"* — a premise MOO-210's R4 withdrew.** Options (1), (2) and (3) of that card are carried here verbatim and unchanged.

     **(A) MOO-210 R1 — FATAL, and it is the fourth defect found in one sentence.** *"The effective date of which the Board determines"* has no referent in Chapter 1: §§ 51.1-153 A/B/E and 51.1-156 A/B/D put the effective date in the member's **own written notification**, §§ 51.1-165 C 1 and 51.1-165.01 F speak of the Board *receiving* notification of it, and §1 itself gives the *stating* of it to the member three times and calls it *"the member's **actual** effective date of retirement."* So on the whole-act reading the exception approved as **f1-b** never fires and [MOO-189](/MOO/issues/MOO-189)'s fatal F1 is uncured — a widower takes a ~$92,600 refund instead of an allowance worth ~$260,000, decided by an administrative date chosen after his wife was dead. **Two forms are drafted and I will not pick one, because every cure is a fourth form of the condition and your standing direction A1 forecloses two of the three families.**
     - **`r1-paid`** — the reviewer's own recommendation and **the only form plainly inside A1**, because it is a fact about *payment* and not about any Board act, filing, notification, acceptance or receipt. Delete the trigger and condition the exception instead: <u>*"except that a retirement that is not a qualifying retirement and under which no payment of a retirement allowance had been made to the member before the date of death is not a retirement before the date of death for the purposes of this subdivision"*</u>. **This is f1-a's substance, tightened from f1-a's own *"has become payable"* to *"paid"* — the reviewer is clear that *"become payable"* would re-open F1, because where the effective date is fixed before the death an allowance arguably became payable for the intervening month.** You chose f1-b over f1-a on 12 October, which is why this comes back to you rather than being drafted in. **It deletes 71-A.**
     - **`r1-approve`** — define the trigger as the Board's act in **approving** the retirement: <u>*"the effective date of a retirement that the Board approves after the date of the member's death"*</u>. It is the administrative reading made express, and it is the reading the whole class actually turns on. **MOO-210 reads A1 as reaching it, because an approval is an acceptance event wearing a different verb — so checking this one releases A1 for this sentence and you should say so expressly.** It keeps 71-A, which is what stops a post-death *correction* firing the branch.

     **(B) The greater-of sentence — R3, R4, R7 with F6, F7 and F18. One question, because they are all in one sentence and cutting it twice is how a cure gets reversed.** R3: subdivision C 2 of § 51.1-165 **names no payee** — it names routes — so *"the person or persons to whom that subdivision requires a benefit to be paid"* has three candidate sets (the contingent annuitant; § 51.1-163's payees, who are § 51.1-162 **A**'s; § 51.1-162 **B**'s), and they pick opposite branches on the same facts. On the worked case a member's mother gets a ~$195,000 allowance or nothing, on the Board's unguided choice between two defensible readings.
     - **`g-payee-163`** — name the sets by the provisions that identify them, which resolves it on the textually right reading: <u>*"the person or persons to whom the benefit that subdivision provides is payable, determined in accordance with § 51.1-163 or subsection B of § 51.1-162 as the case may be, and the contingent annuitant is not that person for this purpose unless that provision so requires"*</u>.
     - **`g-payee-annuitant`** — resolve it on the reading a reader of C 2 alone will reach: <u>*"the contingent annuitant or annuitants under the option elected"*</u>. **Alternative to `g-payee-163`.**
     - **`g-pay-both`** — delete the coincidence test and the exclusion branch and pay each provision's benefit to its own payees, reducing neither. **This reverses f4-a, which you approved on 12 October**, and it is the structurally clean answer to **R4**: f4-a is not a neutral confinement but a reduction of about **$172,000** in present value for a class defined by nothing more than mixed-age children and no surviving spouse — a six-year-old daughter loses her § 51.1-162 B allowance so that two children split a ~$62,000 refund. **No person is worse off than under present law** (the reviewer constructed the non-coincident case four ways and confirmed it), **but nothing in the fiscal record carries the reduction against the design the Lab recommends.**
     - **`g-r7-operate`** — **cumulative, and needed only if f4-a's exclusion stands.** R7: the prohibition is aimed at a § 51.1-142.4 artifact (*"the benefit determined under the preceding sentence"*) and does not displace § 51.1-162 B, which says the allowance *"shall be paid"* and is not conditioned on § 51.1-165 C. Operate on the entitlement: <u>*"no benefit is payable under § 51.1-162 on the member's death in that case"*</u>. **Weigh this before checking it: it switches off a § 51.1-162 entitlement with no *"notwithstanding"* behind it, so it extends the act's reach into a section outside its footprint for a fourth time, and [MOO-207](/MOO/issues/MOO-207)'s authority answer does not cover it.** If you take `g-pay-both` this option is moot.
     - **`g-basis-124.3`** — F6 and F18 together, and the cheapest thing on this card. The act names three different formulations for the actuarial basis and **never uses the chapter's own defined term**: § 51.1-124.3 defines *"actuarial equivalent"* by *"actuarial tables **adopted** by the Board"*, which is an act with a record, where the enacted words key to what the Board *"uses"*, attribute to § 51.1-165 a determination that section does not assign to the Board, and name a set that **contains no factor for discounting a lump sum** — and the comparison has a lump sum on one side. Replace with <u>*"as actuarial equivalents within the meaning of § 51.1-124.3"*</u>. **Compatible with every other option in this group.**

     **(C) MOO-200 F2 — the terminus, and it needs one number from you.** On the words as they stand **f7-a bars the Commonwealth's payment for ever** in the ordinary case: § 51.1-162 B fixes no deadline for the in-lieu election, so where an allowance is payable and the survivor simply takes it and never elects, the determination f7-a waits on can never be made. The $74,685 is then settled, charged against the $3,000,000 and unreleasable, owed by subsection N's first sentence and unpayable by its second. **`f2-90`** — bar the payment only until the earlier of the Board's determination and <u>*"the ninetieth day after the notice required by this subdivision is given on the member's death"*</u>, and treat an election made after the payment as a case for the reinstatement machinery subdivision G 4 already has. **The numeral would be mine, which is why it is here: 90 days is the period § 51.1-162 C already uses and the period the G 4 notice already runs on.**

     **(D) MOO-200 F5 — the § 51.1-162 C waiver period is indefinitely revivable and nothing can be recovered.** The extension sentence is keyed to *"a notice required by this subdivision"* — **any** of them — and store revision 54's further-notice duty has no outer date, so every re-determination restarts the waiver period, while subsection F and subdivision G 4 both forbid collecting back anything already paid. On the worked case the superseded branch has paid about **$69,000** that the Board may not recover. **`f5-outer`** — key the extension to <u>*"the notice first required by this subdivision on the member's death"*</u>, add that the period <u>*"shall not in any event expire later than one year after the date of death"*</u>, and add a reconciliation rule for a switch between subsections B and C of § 51.1-162 after payment has begun. **Two numerals and a new rule, so all of it is yours.**

     **(E) MOO-200 F4 — the § 401(a)(37) qualifier, and this is a fork between two counsel passes on one clause.** MOO-189's **F7** said the certification stated § 51.1-162 B's conclusion **without** the federal qualifier and must carry it; store revision 58 enacted it; MOO-200's **F4** says that enacting it was harmful, because read literally the opening words make the whole of § 51.1-162 B operative *"to the extent required by § 401(a)(37)"* — which, for a member who dies at her desk rather than on active duty, is **to no extent at all** — and the act's own certification is now the best evidence a litigant has that the Board does not think the subsection reaches an ordinary death. **The two findings point opposite ways and only you can settle which risk the act carries.**
     - **`f4-drop`** — drop the quoted qualifier and leave the plain description with the subsection cited, so the member reads § 51.1-162 B for herself. **Reverses MOO-189 F7.**
     - **`f4-state`** — keep the qualifier and add <u>*"and that the Board treats subsection B of § 51.1-162 as providing that allowance on the death in service of any member"*</u>. **Alternative to `f4-drop`. It is a statement about how the Board reads a federal provision, compelled in writing — which is why I have not drafted it in.**

     **(F) MOO-200 F9 — m8-a's unit.** Clause (i) already stops a member manufacturing tolling by re-filing on the **same** determination, so the 90-day aggregate you approved on 12 October bites only across **different** determinations — it penalises the member for the Board's lateness on matters she had a statutory right to have reconsidered separately, and it is exhausted at the **fourth** distinct first-time request, after which her final qualifying retirement date can arrive while a reconsideration is live and subdivision G 2 takes back about three of her four purchased years. **`f9-per-request`** — make the exclusion <u>*"per request"*</u> rather than aggregate: *"the period so excluded in respect of any one request shall not exceed 90 days."* **This is the same incidence question you decided against route (b) on, arriving from the other side**, and it loosens a bound you set eight days ago, which is why it is a card.

     **(G) MOO-200 F11 — a non-member's window runs from a document he never receives.** Store revision 67's B4 restored subdivision M 2's deeming sentence for non-members, which was directionally right and does not close the gap: both of the non-member's routes are keyed to documents a non-member may never see — subdivision D 1's window runs from a **G 4 notice**, which exists only where the member dies, and the deeming route runs from the notice of a certification given *"to the member"*. A state agency that paid $12,000 from its appropriation discovers the defect eight months later and its 30 days expired seven months before. **`f11-agency`** — run the non-member's window from <u>*"the earlier of the notice required by subdivision G 4 and the date the Board gives that person notice of the certification"*</u> **and** require the Board to give the certification to any state agency from whose appropriation an amount was paid under subdivision B 4 of § 51.1-142.2. **The second limb is a new duty on the Board and the first does not work without it**, which is why neither is drafted in.

     **(H) MOO-210 R8 and MOO-200 F14 — carried verbatim from the withdrawn card, unchanged.** R8 reports that store revision 69's `f14-move` moved F14 **backwards on both limbs**: in subsection L the untimed bar read as a statement about categories of persons and the argument was weak, and in subsection B it now stands immediately after *"An eligible member may purchase service credit … if, and only if"* — the operative permission, in the present tense — while *"eligible member"* opens *"means a member **in service** who…"*. **The named loser is MOO-169 F1's own class, the abolished-position member**, told at retirement that she was never an eligible member and so never eligible to elect.
     - **`f14-anchor`** — give the bar a time anchor at the filing date of the complete application: <u>*"determined as of the filing date of that person's complete application"*</u>. **This is R8's own first fix direction and it is now more urgent than when it was first offered, because of the new adjacency.** Cumulative with either option below.
     - **`f14-L`** — give subsection L an express second sentence for its own audience (VML, VACo and the Commission on Local Government, which under § 30-19.03 must prepare a local-fiscal estimate). **Cumulative with `f14-move`; reverses nothing you decided.**
     - **`f14-reverse`** — put the bar back in subsection L and undo `f14-move`. **Alternative to `f14-L`.**

     **What is not on this card, and why.** MOO-210's **R10** and **R11** are declined with reasons at §6.0-71 — R10's inoperative first limb is harmless and deleting it would change words you approved for no operative gain, and R11's residual leaves the retiree regime in place, which is the outcome MOO-161's and MOO-169's fatal findings were cured to achieve. **MOO-200's F15 and F16 and R9 are enacted in store revision 71 without asking**, because a title line naming an object the act does not contain, a record pointer rendered as a floating-point number, and a summary table attributing a provision to the wrong subsection are defects of accuracy and not choices. **F1's certification half, F3, F8, F10, F12, F13 and F17 are enacted for the same reason.**

137. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 73, out of the cross-reference sweep for this revision (§5.49 check 4) — drafted in one form and enacted nowhere. The cure you approved for F3 narrows the subsection M channel and does not close it.** You approved S1 in form M-a and it is enacted. **It excepts the advice failure from subsection M 2's failure-to-state limb. M 2's second sentence independently deems a *certification* a determination of the Board** — *"Every determination, **certification**, redetermination, recertification, reduction, cancellation, rescission, restoration, crediting of service credit, placement on a waiting list, expiration of a reservation, denial of an election, and refusal to accept an election under this section is a determination of the Board for the purposes of this subsection, whether or not another provision of this section expressly provides for review of it."* So a member whose certification omits the advice may still seek reconsideration as a person *"adversely affected by a determination of the Board under this section"*, on the determination clock and not on subdivision D 1's.

    **Why this is a route and not a reading, in the act's own words.** Subdivision D 1 disapplies *"the second sentence of subdivision M 2"* for the member. **That disapplication exists for no other reason than that the determination limb is otherwise available** — if the deeming sentence did not reach a certification's content, there would be nothing to disapply. And it disapplies the sentence only *"to such a failure"*, which after F3 and S1 **no longer includes the advice failure**. The one failure you have twice decided should not be reviewable is therefore the one failure for which the deeming sentence is left in force. *(The contrary reading is respectable: a specific exception controls a general deeming, and a court would be slow to let the determination limb restore a review the General Assembly had just excepted. The text does not say so.)*

    **The cure, form M-c. +216, no markup, one sentence, in subsection M 2 after the deeming sentence.** FIND: *"is a determination of the Board for the purposes of this subsection, whether or not another provision of this section expressly provides for review of it."* — 154 characters, resolving **1× inside the §1 slice** of the cut text. READ: that sentence followed by *"A certification is not a determination of the Board for the purposes of this subsection by reason only of a failure to state in it that the Board advises the member to review the member's designation of beneficiary."* **The cure leaves `<u>`/`</u>` at 62 / 62** and requires **no conforming amendment anywhere**: the five declaratory *"is a determination of the Board for the purposes of subsection M"* clauses were each checked and **none of them reaches a certification**, so the deeming of a certification lives only in this sentence.

    **Why it is not cut.** It is a further opening of subsection M, which is yours and not mine, and the words are not among the six you approved. **The alternative is to leave it**: the exception then reads as narrowing one limb of M 2 while the other limb is open, which is defensible and which no reader of subsection M can see. §6.0-73 item 3. Read with item 135.

138. **NEW IN STORE REVISION 73, out of the cross-reference sweep for this revision (§5.49 check 5) — drafted in one form and enacted nowhere. The saving clause you approved for the frozen descriptions does not reach the subdivision G 4 death notice, which is the one document a survivor ever receives.** You approved S5 and it is enacted at +310. It freezes *"a statement **this subdivision** requires"* of the effect of a provision of § 51.1-162, § 51.1-163, § 51.1-165 or § 51.1-165.01 to the provision **as it reads on the date the certification is given**. **Subdivision D 1 is not the only provision of the section that compels such a description.**

    **Subdivision G 4 compels three of them, in the notice the Board gives on the member's death:** the right under **subsection C of § 51.1-162** of a beneficiary entitled to the entire allowance to waive it; **the period that subsection states within which the waiver must be made**, together with any extension of it under G 4; and the right under **subsection B of § 51.1-162** of the person to whom an allowance is payable to elect the member's accumulated contributions instead. **None of the three is frozen.** If § 51.1-162 C were amended to change the waiver period, the Board would be compelled by G 4 to state the new period and would have no saving clause — and the person reading that notice is a survivor acting on a deadline, not a member with years in hand. **The defect S5 cures survives in the worse place.**

    **The cure, form S5-w. +48 over S5 as enacted, no markup, and it replaces S5's own sentence rather than adding one.** FIND: S5's sentence as enacted, 310 characters, resolving **1×** in the cut text. READ: *"A statement **this section** requires of the effect of a provision of § 51.1-162, of § 51.1-163, of § 51.1-165, or of § 51.1-165.01 is a statement of that provision as it reads on the date **the statement, notice, or certification** is given, and a statement that conforms to that provision as it then reads satisfies **the provision of this section that requires it**."* Three changes, each mechanical: the subdivision becomes the section; the date is supplied by **the act's own compound *"that statement, notice, or certification"***, which subsection A already carries; and the satisfaction clause follows. **No conforming amendment anywhere**, and the sentence stays in subdivision D 1, where the compelled descriptions are densest.

    **Why it is not cut.** The words you approved are enacted, and widening them reaches a provision — subdivision G 4 — that the MOO-208 commission did not put in front of you. **The alternative is a parallel sentence in G 4**, which this drafter does not recommend: two constructions of one concept in two places is how the chapter's vocabulary rule gets broken. §6.0-73 item 4.

### 7.1 Retained record — performed; moved to the retained-record document in store revision 41

**Moved verbatim** to [`bill-text-s8-revision-history`](/MOO/issues/MOO-42#document-bill-text-s8-revision-history), every numbered item, table row and lettered label preserved, so a reference of the form **§7.1** still resolves there. Same reason and method as the sections moved in store revisions 34, 36 and 38: the file was 3,555 characters from the store's 524,288-character ceiling and store revision 41's record did not fit.

**The durable results.** It is the record of the numbers and choices that were mine rather than the Director's, moved to the end of §7 in store revision 33 on pass 9's m-6 and not amended since. **It is a retained record and it is now in the retained-record document, which is the only move in this revision that is pure housekeeping.** Its durable result is the standing principle it exists to serve: **every number this draft chose rather than received is recorded with its direction of effect**, and nothing in it is operative text, a drafted option, or an open question. A reference of the form **§7.1 item 3** still resolves there, and §7's numbered items 1 to 70 are unaffected.
